### Chaise interior — development/commons_109923183

Step 2 constructs architecture from boxes and a chaise from apron boxes, swept legs and borders, plus separately meshed upholstered wings, head and a 64-point-per-ring cushion. A rotated pillow, piping, label, curtains and cropped cane chair complete the composition. These are constructed parts, not verified physical connections [X0539, `steps/002/build.py`, lines 22–100, 162–220, 238–278]. Procedural wave/ramp and noise/bump create striped fabric; rotated brick and multiplied waves are intended as parquet and cane [X0539, lines 119–146, 280–335].

Step 3 retains the furniture but moves the wall to y=3 and turns it 20 degrees, and relocates the foreground chair. Its comments describe landmark estimates and an intended correction, not calibrated measurements or proof of improved fidelity [X0541, `steps/003/build.py`, lines 4–20, 26–61, 117–133]. Both use camera (1, −5.4, 3.65), 57 mm lens, two area lights and AgX; step 3 specifies exposure +0.15 [X0541, lines 16–18, 135–142, 379–386]. Both results report Blender 4.2.10, 16/16 samples, saved PNG and blend, `ok: true`, null error and empty stderr [X0540, `steps/002/result.json`, lines 2–7; X0542, `steps/003/result.json`, lines 2–7]. The task specifies a 512×384 reference, not independently measured render geometry [X0543, `task.json`, lines 2–5]. No selection or acceptance for this scene is supplied.

### Ship compartment — development/commons_118588033

## Historical execution audit

**Scope and provenance.** The supplied record contains a step 001 execution result and a step 002 generated script, not the latter’s execution result or either image. References below use the original expanded files and source IDs:

- **X0130:** `/srv/lab-disks/disk2/users/xymo/Documents/Codex/2026-11-02/scene-rsi-rsi-1-3d-blender/outputs/scene_rsi_pilot/results/ablation_r1/generic_same_structured_evidence/C1/development/commons_109923183/steps/001/result.json`.
- **X0131:** the same expanded directory, `steps/002/build.py`.
- Both are recorded as members of `/srv/lab-disks/disk2/users/xymo/Documents/Codex/2026-11-02/scene-rsi-rsi-1-3d-blender/work/scene_archive/bundles/ablation_r1--generic_same_structured_evidence.tar.gz`, under `experiments/scene_rsi_pilot/results/ablation_r1/generic_same_structured_evidence/C1/development/commons_109923183/steps/001/result.json` and `steps/002/build.py`, respectively.

**Code intent and representation.** Step 002’s comments report an earlier review: the chaise was too small and rightward, wall lines sloped too much, and floor and upholstery were washed out. These are recorded claims, not independently observed visual findings (X0131, lines 6–20). The script constructs a paneled room and many individually represented parquet boards, then an antique chaise with a rounded seat and underframe, swept gilt edges and apron, four curved legs and feet, head and arm frames, ornaments, a tilted pillow and a small card. A foreground chair has a cane grid; curtains are repeated rods (X0131, lines 27–80, 91–212). Three separately created polygonal upholstery panels receive solidify and bevel modifiers (lines 286–307). Their coordinates express placement and intended connections; they do **not** establish physical contact or image fidelity.

**Materials and texture operations.** After `realize(scene)`, the script creates node-based ivory textiles: position-driven high-frequency noise feeds a small bump, while two directional wave bands, thresholds and multiplication mix ivory and muted red for repeated dashes. It assigns these to named seat and pillow descendants and uses the patterned material on the three panels (X0131, lines 214–216, 218–284, 301–307). Twelve walnut materials use generated coordinates scaled anisotropically into noise and bump; parquet descendants receive random palette choices (lines 313–340). These are script operations, not proof of their appearance in a successful step 002 render.

**Pose, camera and render intent.** The pillow is rotated −18° about Y and −12° about X; the chair is rotated −13° about Z (X0131, lines 174–179, 189–204). The camera is specified at `(1.22, -4.90, 3.65)`, aimed at `(.12, .08, .96)`, with 48 mm lens and −5° roll. Two area emitters have powers 390 and 65; the world background strength is 0.20. Intended output is 512 × 384 PNG, Cycles, 48 samples, denoising, AgX and −0.25 exposure (lines 12–17, 342–366).

**Actual execution and validation.** Step 001 reports `ok: true`, `error: null`, empty stderr, Blender 4.2.10 LTS, completion of 16/16 samples, and saving both `final.png` and `scene.blend` (X0130, lines 2–7). Its synchronization log includes numerous Box, Rod and Ball objects and named upholstered arms and head end, but does not provide measured dimensions or prove their connections (line 5). The 16 samples are measured **step 001** render metadata, not confirmation of step 002’s 48-sample settings. Step 002’s “API audit” is a code comment, not an independently recorded validation result (X0131, lines 214–216).

**Selection and quality.** No recorded selection, acceptance score, teacher feedback, step 002 execution error or validation result is supplied. Step 001 was technically completed, rather than a demonstrated rendering failure; its reconstruction quality remains unverified because neither render nor reference image is available here. Step 002 may be an attempted improvement, but its successful execution and visual quality cannot be established from its script alone.

**Actual execution versus revision.** Step 1 reports `ok: true`, `error: null`, and empty stderr. Blender 4.2.10 LTS reached sample 16/16, reported the render finished, saved `final.png` and `scene.blend` (X0496, lines 2–6). This is a completed technical render, not evidence of visual fidelity. Its comments claim step 1 occupied about 49% of image width versus a 79% target, but these are revision-author assessments, not independently measured image metadata (X0497, lines 5–13).

Cushions are custom smoothed superellipsoid-like meshes; the near side is a single polygon with solidify and bevel modifiers, while a separate mesh and small cubes form a label and printing (lines 206–264). Coordinates and shared assembly transforms do **not** prove physical contact, absence of intersections, or image fidelity.

**Materials and texture operations.** The revision uses color-valued primitives and node-based Principled materials. Upholstery feeds generated Y coordinates through multiplication, sine and a color ramp to create stripes, with noise-driven bump; the pillow has separate noise bump. These are procedural operations, not evidence of a loaded image texture (X0497, lines 23–29, 167–204).

**Pose and rendering setup.** The revision specifies camera `(0.30, -4.0, 3.20)`, target `(-0.15, 0.05, 0.96)`, 48 mm lens and a −12° chaise rotation; extra upholstery is transformed and parented to the chaise if its root exists. It sets a blue-gray world at strength 0.19, two warm area emitters at powers 420 and 45, and AgX view transform (X0497, lines 14–20, 266–292). Step 1's log measures 16 samples and approximately 6.65 seconds including saving, but does not establish the step 2 settings or a comparable image size (X0496, line 5).

**Validation, selection and quality.** The step 2 file ends with an audit comment asserting positive dimensions, unique keys and distinct tube points; it is not a recorded validation result. It leaves rendering and saving to the runner (X0497, lines 293–295). No selection, acceptance, teacher feedback, or independent visual-quality assessment is recorded in the supplied sources. With no images provided, neither the claimed discrepancy nor the reconstruction's appearance can be verified; a technically completed step 1 could still be a poor reconstruction.

### Electronics workbench — commons_16007442

The requested engine is EEVEE Next [X0188, lines 14–27, 303–329]. The step-3 log’s 16 samples are observed render metadata, distinct from the script’s engine setting. No selection, acceptance or independent visual validation for this scene is supplied.

### Dormitory — commons_163096530

**Code intent and representation.** The final build models 100 individually colored floorboards, walls and timbers; three composite bunks with posts, rails, ladders, rounded mattresses and pillows; a wardrobe, fireplace, radiator and other details [X0191, `commons_163096530/build.py`, lines 37–110, 112–174]. It positions and vertically scales the foreground bunk for intended cropping. Code coordinates do not establish contact between parts or fidelity to the photograph.

The recorded review of step 2 claims misplaced rear furniture, a missing cropped bunk and overly light floor and ceiling; the final code changes camera position and target, repositions and scales that bunk, and darkens surfaces. These are the author’s observations and intended corrections, not independently verified image findings [X0191, lines 5–21, 106–110]. Step 1 instead specified a 26 mm lens and a different camera target; step 2 specified 34 mm and moved the foreground bunk outward [X0195, `commons_163096530/steps/001/build.py`, lines 6–17, 110–115; X0197, `commons_163096530/steps/002/build.py`, lines 6–18, 99–102].

**Materials and render settings.** The final script copies material slots before local edits, then links generated coordinates through scaled noise and a color ramp into base color, with noise-driven bump for wood, plaster and stone. Dark bunk materials receive metallic 0.68 and roughness 0.29 [X0191, lines 218–273]. It specifies a 34 mm camera, two area lights, Cycles at 48 samples with denoising, 512 × 341, and AgX exposure 0.10 [X0191, lines 13–18, 204–210, 275–287].

**Execution and validation.** Step 1 actually reports a finished 16/16-sample render, saved PNG and blend, `ok: true` and empty stderr; the observed sample count differs from its script’s requested 48 [X0196, `commons_163096530/steps/001/result.json`, lines 2–7; X0195, lines 277–284]. The supplied record reports the overall run complete on CPU, three executions and no runtime error, but does not provide individual step-2 or step-3 render logs [X0192, `commons_163096530/record.json`, lines 16–18, 37–53]. `selected.json` records step 3, while the record’s `selected` field is null [X0193, `commons_163096530/selected.json`, lines 1–3; X0192, line 57].

The record reports valid and functional passes, overall 0.4063, SSIM 0.1439 and LPIPS 0.7633. Its functional interpretation explicitly limits the test to structural/edit operability; semantic completeness and support/collision truth are null [X0192, lines 17–34]. These numbers and selection are recorded outcomes, not independent acceptance of visual quality. No reference or rendered images are attached here, so visual fidelity cannot be assessed. The documented successful renders are technically completed, not evidence of rendering failure; a completed reconstruction may nevertheless be poor.

## Bedroom: three completed renders

The first script’s comments specify an intended canopy, overlapping bench, paneled door, foreground carpet and cropped chair—not measured image results. The code constructs separate assets from boxes, rods, balls and swept tubes: layered bed, posts, scallops and curtain folds; furniture, lamps, carpet borders and landscape decoration. These coordinates express placement, not proof of contact or fidelity. [X1281, `steps/001/build.py`, lines 5–10, 26–101, 107–190]

After realization, step 1 attempts name-based wall noise, carpet Voronoi color and upholstery bump, with roughness 0.78. Its selectors depend on realized mesh names; successful rendering alone does not establish that every intended material was matched. Step 2 explicitly notes generic realized names and instead matches shader colors, adding wall noise, carpet Voronoi, textile noise/bump and shade emission. [X1281, `steps/001/build.py`, lines 192–248; X1283, `steps/002/build.py`, lines 195–267]

Step 2 attributes the revision to wall occlusion, small canopy and pale textiles: it shortens the right wall and moves the camera from `(5.5,-9.2,3.15)`, 47 mm, to `(3.45,-7,2.85)`, 43 mm. Step 3 reports remaining framing and occlusion problems, moves the foreground chair, deepens colors, changes the target and lens to 49 mm, and reduces the main emitter from 650 to 470. These are author observations and changes, not independent visual validation. [X1281, `steps/001/build.py`, lines 250–264; X1283, `steps/002/build.py`, lines 5–7, 24–29, 269–284; X1285, `steps/003/build.py`, lines 5–17, 127–140, 271–286]

The reference metadata is 512×340; each script sets that render size, and step 1 explicitly requests PNG. All three results report `ok: true`, null error, empty stderr, 16/16 samples, saved `final.png` and `scene.blend`, using Blender 4.2.10. Thus these are completed renders, not technical render failures; no bedroom selection or acceptance is supplied. [X1289, `record.json`, lines 17–34]

## Metalworking workshop

**Code intent and representation.** The selected step-3 build groups editable boxes, rods and 24-segment swept rings into floorboards, windows, a long timber bench, lathe, flywheel, anvil, vises, drawer and inventory. The code comments describe a prior render as too distant and pale; step 3 moves the camera to (5, −4.2, 3.65), targets (−0.80, 0.15, 1.12), retains a 43 mm lens and reduces daylight/fill to 450/65. Step 3 likewise reports success, empty stderr, a saved PNG and blend file; its render log records 16 samples and 20.46 seconds. The log’s render behavior should not be conflated with the build’s Eevee setting: it is separately reported execution evidence [X0175, T/commons_155212263/steps/002/build.py, lines 31–48; X0176, T/commons_155212263/steps/002/result.json, lines 2–7; X0178, T/commons_155212263/steps/003/result.json, lines 2–7].

Its overall score is 0.4325, SSIM 0.1454 and LPIPS 0.7022—not independent proof of fidelity [X0171, T/commons_155212263/selected.json, line 2; X0170, T/commons_155212263/record.json, lines 16–34].

## Kitchen — selection/commons_133800864

The recorded build represents blinds, cabinet, dispenser, towel/ring, sink, faucet, blender and separate vessels with boxes, rods, ellipsoids and tubes. The blender jar has opacity .23; the pitcher’s purported clear shell is colored geometry, with translucency uncertain (X0683, `build.py`, lines 88–152, 173–224). No per-step result log for this scene is supplied, so its recorded completion does not independently document the individual render process.

## Execution audit

**Provenance key.** All citations below identify original expanded files under `/srv/lab-disks/disk2/users/xymo/Documents/Codex/2026-11-02/scene-rsi-rsi-1-3d-blender/outputs/scene_rsi_pilot/results/r2/generic_rsi/C2/`. Their corresponding archive is `/srv/lab-disks/disk2/users/xymo/Documents/Codex/2026-11-02/scene-rsi-rsi-1-3d-blender/work/scene_archive/bundles/r2--generic_rsi.tar.gz`; each member is `experiments/scene_rsi_pilot/results/r2/generic_rsi/C2/` followed by the cited relative file path. Line numbers are those of the original expanded files; result logs are serialized on single lines.

### Chaise room — development/commons_109923183

Step 2 actually rendered: Blender 4.2.10 completed 16 samples, saved `final.png` and `scene.blend`, and reported 1,400 scene solids, including 883 parquet and 419 chaise solids. The result reports no error or stderr ([X1022, `steps/002/result.json`, lines 2–7]). The reference metadata is 512 × 384, not a measurement of the rendered image ([X1025, `task.json`, lines 2–5]).

Step 3’s **code intent**, not a confirmed completed render, was to raise the camera, reveal the mattress top, broaden the pillow and shift architecture back. It builds parquet from individual boxes; a filled chaise from filleted cushions, segmented back and wings, swept rails and four curved supports; and separate pillow, card and foreground chair assets ([X1023, `steps/003/build.py`, lines 4–14, 58–72, 118–235]). It applies noise bump and rose bands to matched upholstery, linen bump to the pillow, and crossed wave textures to the cane seat. Camera is `(-.72,-5.65,4.55)`, lens 68, with yaw −29°, two area lights and AgX ([X1023, lines 38–45, 259–350]). The membership assertions check nonempty semantic assets, not contact or fidelity ([X1023, lines 237–247]).

The revision timed out after approximately 177.9 seconds with empty stdout/stderr and no image. The record does not establish which operations completed before timeout or its cause ([X1024, `steps/003/result.json`, lines 2–7]). This is a technical execution failure, not evidence that the earlier completed image was visually good or bad.

### Ship compartment — development/commons_118588033

The generated scene uses grouped boxes, rods and spheres for the compartment, three berths and supports, cabinets, chair, pedestal, pipes, board, emergency station and lamps. Step 3 moves the berth as a group and turns the chair −13°; its assertions require named, nonempty assets, rather than testing connections ([X1034, `steps/003/build.py`, lines 70–138, 200–229, 315–334]). It sets a 28 mm camera at `(.05,-4.70,1.64)`, four area emitters, AgX and exposure −0.80. Palette materials receive roughness edits; copied floor and lamp materials receive local roughness and emission edits, with matching assertions ([X1034, lines 39–53, 334–399]).

Steps 1, 2 and 3 all report successful 16-sample renders and saved images and blend files. Their logged solid counts were respectively 216, 228 and 230; step 3 includes seven pedestal solids and two doorway solids ([X1031, `steps/001/result.json`, lines 2–7; X1033, `steps/002/result.json`, lines 2–7; X1035, `steps/003/result.json`, lines 2–7]). The task specifies a 384 × 512 reference ([X1036, `task.json`, lines 2–5]). `selected.json` records step 3, although `record.json` itself has `selected: null` ([X1028, `selected.json`, line 2; X1027, `record.json`, line 57]).

The record marks the run complete and valid, with SSIM 0.1912 and overall 0.4113; functional checks pass for the pedestal, but are explicitly structural/edit-operability checks. Semantic completeness and support-collision truth are null ([X1027, `record.json`, lines 16–34]). Thus successful rendering and functional passes do not establish physical contact or visual reconstruction quality.

### Kitchen — selection/commons_133800864

The generated build represents the cabinet, blinds, ring and folded towel as separate grouped primitives; subtracts a sink opening from the counter; and places a blender, jug and pitcher independently. It uses procedural granite noise/ramp/bump, towel and paper bump, copied metallic and plastic finishes, a 66 mm camera, two area lights and AgX ([X1037, `build.py`, lines 34–40, 77–89, 128–162, 224–285, 312–402]). Step 3 is recorded as selected; the run record says complete and valid, with SSIM 0.2967 and overall 0.4588, and functional checks passing for the blender. Again, completeness and collision truth are null and the checks concern operability ([X1039, `selected.json`, line 2; X1038, `record.json`, lines 16–34, 49–57]). The supplied sources do not include its individual render log or images for visual inspection. Neither these scores nor selection independently validate image fidelity; coordinates alone cannot prove mounting, support or contact.

### Window-side kitchen — commons_133800864

The generated scene uses boxes for walls, cabinet doors and 32 tilted blind slats; rods, balls and swept tubes represent the roll, ring, tap and containers. The counter has a Boolean sink cutout; the towel is a collection of separate narrow panels, not simulated cloth. The pitcher and jug are assemblies with specified opacity values (X1237, `r2/sceneevolve_cli/C2/selection/commons_133800864/steps/001/build.py`, lines 22–43, 61–108, 111–145).

Step 2 raised the cabinet bottom to 1.465 and widened the lens from 57 to 49; step 3 shortened the roll, reoriented the ring and towel, moved the jug by (-.16,.04,0), and reduced exposure to -1.15. Granite-matching materials receive noise at scale 185 and a color ramp; chrome-matching materials receive metallic .8 and roughness .25. Although step 1 mentions paper grain, the shown node operations implement stone and chrome only. The camera is at (-1.10,-2.25,1.80), with two area lights and a Standard view transform (X1237, `steps/001/build.py`, lines 156–196). No selection or acceptance record for this scene is supplied.

The selected step-3 code builds a box-based island with drawers, timber posts, ceiling beams, dark cabinetry, ovens and a suspended hood. Its comments claim the wall correction worked and describe remaining reference/render differences; the images are not supplied for independent visual assessment (X1250, `steps/002/build.py`, lines 10–20, 29–32; X1252, `steps/003/build.py`, lines 4–16).

### Archive corridor — commons_2238909

The replay’s selected step is 3 (X1257, `replay_s1/H_start/H_start/replay_development/commons_2238909/selected.json`, lines 1–3). ### Electronics workbench — technical failure

The generated SceneAct code represents the bench and cabinets with boxes, instruments as multipart assets, and cables as swept tubes; the monitor, meter and keyboard receive explicit rotations. These are construction intentions, not verified connections or image fidelity (X0463, `commons_16007442/steps/002/build.py`, lines 13–49, 56–92, 112–143, 230–249). Its intended camera is `(0.65,-2.83,2.62)`, lens 44, with two area lights and Cycles at 32 samples, 512×388, AgX (X0463, lines 314–326).

Step 1 actually stopped at the mouse: radius 0.027 exceeded half its smallest dimension, 0.023; no image was returned (X0461, `commons_16007442/steps/001/build.py`, line 146; X0462, `commons_16007442/steps/001/result.json`, lines 2–7). Step 2 reduced the radius to 0.020 but timed out after approximately 600 seconds, with no diagnostic output or image; the cause and execution progress are unknown (X0463, line 143; X0464, `commons_16007442/steps/002/result.json`, lines 2–7). Step 3 batched details and used Blender curves, but failed when the scene world was `None`, before its render settings were reached (X0465, `commons_16007442/steps/003/build.py`, lines 135–152, 264–277, 365–382; X0466, `commons_16007442/steps/003/result.json`, lines 2–7). The requested blue workbench edit was not performed: status is `reconstruction_failed`, with zero editor calls (X0468, `commons_16007442/text_edit/status.json`, lines 2–5).

### Dormitory — rendered, quality not established

Step 1 failed on a missing world (X0474, `commons_163096530/steps/001/result.json`, lines 2–7). Step 2 created one and **actually rendered and saved** `final.png` and `scene.blend`; the render log reports 16/16 samples, not the 48 specified in the build, so the effective render differed from code intent (X0475, lines 262–267, 278–284; X0476, `commons_163096530/steps/002/result.json`, lines 2–7). Cupboard structure/material/composite checks pass, explicitly for edit operability; semantic completeness and support/collision truth are null (X0470, lines 16–34). ### Woodworking museum — recorded complete, limited execution evidence

# Historical execution audit

**Provenance.** Paths below are relative to `/srv/lab-disks/disk2/users/xymo/Documents/Codex/2026-11-02/scene-rsi-rsi-1-3d-blender/`. Expanded files are under `outputs/scene_rsi_pilot/results/`; their archive members have the same suffix under `experiments/scene_rsi_pilot/results/`. The r1 sources are in `work/scene_archive/bundles/r1--sceneevolve_cli.tar.gz`; r2 sources are in `work/scene_archive/bundles/r2--fixed_h0.tar.gz`. Citations identify the original file suffix, source ID, and original line numbers. The JSON render logs are each contained on one physical line.

## Workshop: completed renders, unassessed appearance

In `r1/sceneevolve_cli/C2/test/commons_199974832/steps/003/build.py`, the generated code constructs an antique woodworking shop from SceneAct assemblies: boxed walls, window and shelves; repeated wooden and metal planes built from boxes, rods and balls; hanging tools; and a bench with a chest, kettle, bottle, vise, segmented nail bowl and loose clutter [X0884, lines 57–128, 131–225, 236–341, 343–410]. These are intended representations, not evidence that every part touches or looks faithful.

After realization, the code classifies finishes by color, darkens wood and metal, and uses generated coordinates, stretched noise, a color ramp and bump for wood; metal receives different roughness and metallic values [X0884, lines 412–464]. The specified camera is `(0,-3.8,2.10)` toward `(-.08,1.5,1.90)`, 27 mm, with three area lights, ambient world, AgX and exposure 0.15 [X0884, lines 8–10, 466–481]. The task metadata gives a **512×384 reference**, not a measured output resolution [X0886, `r1/sceneevolve_cli/C2/test/commons_199974832/task.json`, lines 2–5].

Step 3 reports `ok: true`, empty stderr, 16/16 samples finished, and saves `final.png` and `scene.blend`; step 2 also reports a finished 16-sample render and saved files [X0885, `…/steps/003/result.json`, lines 2–7; X0883, `…/steps/002/result.json`, lines 2–7]. These are technical completion records, not visual validation. No workshop selection or acceptance record is supplied.

## Chaise: technical failure, not a quality judgment

The chaise programs intend a paneled interior, parquet, curtains, and a rotated upholstered chaise with cushion, back, wings, pillow, piping, carved-looking rails and curved legs, plus a foreground cane chair [X0889, `r2/fixed_h0/H0/development/commons_109923183/steps/001/build.py`, lines 24–111, 113–230, 272–301]. They specify procedural fabric noise/bump and rose stripes, a 60 mm camera, two area lights and 512×384 Cycles rendering [X0889, lines 303–393, 405–431]. Those later operations were **not reached in step 1**: a chair-seat fillet radius of 0.05 exceeded half its 0.04 thickness [X0890, `…/steps/001/result.json`, lines 2–7; X0889, lines 275–279].

Step 2 reduced that radius to 0.018 but timed out at approximately 600 seconds with no image; the log does not establish where time was spent [X0891, `…/steps/002/build.py`, lines 282–286; X0892, `…/steps/002/result.json`, lines 2–7]. Step 3 replaced individual parquet objects with one multi-face mesh and used Blender curves for decorative parts, but failed when accessing `s.world.use_nodes` on a null world, before its subsequent render settings [X0893, `…/steps/003/build.py`, lines 121–137, 261–320; X0894, `…/steps/003/result.json`, lines 2–7]. The record reports missing scene/image, invalid metrics, functional failure and no selection; runtime status itself has a null error [X0887, `…/record.json`, lines 16–35, 46–55].

## Ship compartment: completed, with bounded validation

The root `build.py` constructs a shipboard compartment and a three-tier bunk assembly from posts, rails, canvas boxes, rounded mattresses and pillows, alongside machinery, lockers, a chair and lamps [X0896, `r2/fixed_h0/H0/development/commons_118588033/build.py`, lines 31–56, 87–105, 111–171, 181–189]. It assigns emission by color and attempts floor noise/bump; it specifies a 27.5 mm view, five area lights and 384×512 Cycles at 64 samples with denoising [X0896, lines 191–233]. These are code settings, not independently measured geometry or proof of contact.

An earlier step 1 failed on a null world [X0901, `…/steps/001/result.json`, lines 2–7]. The separate selection file records step 3, while the summary’s `selected` field is null [X0898, `…/selected.json`, lines 1–3; X0897, `…/record.json`, lines 53–57]. The summary reports completion, valid metrics and functional passes for bunks, but explicitly limits the functional interpretation to structural/edit operability; semantic completeness and support/collision truth are null. Its overall score is 0.3966, SSIM 0.1634 and LPIPS 0.7992 [X0897, lines 16–35]. This is a completed but potentially poor reconstruction, not a rendering failure or independently established visual quality. No images were supplied for visual inspection.

**Quality limit.** Neither coordinates nor assembled part names prove that rails, upholstery, tools or supports physically connect. No reference or rendered image is attached for visual inspection; numeric scores and operability passes do not independently validate image quality. The documented completed renders must therefore be distinguished from potentially poor reconstruction, not described as technical failures.

## Historical bunk-room execution audit

**Provenance and scope.** The following citations identify supplied expanded files under `/srv/lab-disks/disk2/users/xymo/Documents/Codex/2026-11-02/scene-rsi-rsi-1-3d-blender/outputs/scene_rsi_pilot/results/r1/sceneevolve_cli/C2/test/commons_163096530/`. **Code and representation.** The top-level `build.py` describes a 6.4 × 7.6 × 3.55 room with plaster walls, ceiling, 46 individually constructed floorboard segments and exposed timbers. Three grouped bunk assets comprise rods, feet, caps, decks, mattresses, filleted pillows, sheets, rails and ladders. Separate assets represent a wardrobe, fireplace, radiator and accessories. The fireplace is scaled 1.15 vertically about a floor pivot and moved 0.20 along x. These are authored dimensions and placements, not measured mesh bounds or proof that the components touch or match the reference [X0865, `build.py`, lines 7–12, 23–118, 120–208].

**Materials and view.** After realization, the code classifies materials by base color, rather than object identity. For wood and plaster it links generated coordinates through vector multiplication and noise to a color ramp and bump; it also sets roughness. Dark near-neutral materials receive metallic 0.65 and roughness 0.30. No image-texture operation is present in this build. The camera is at (2.15, −5.35, 1.82), aimed at (−0.30, 2.35, 1.18), with lens 36 and sensor 36. Two area emitters have powers 1050 and 100; world strength is 0.22 and the view transform is AgX [X0865, `build.py`, lines 10–12, 210–271]. These are code settings, not a visual assessment.

**Iteration and execution.** Step 1 used a 30 lens and higher camera target; step 2 changed these to 36 and 1.18 respectively, moved the left bunks and scaled the fireplace [X0869, `steps/001/build.py`, lines 8–14, 118–121; X0871, `steps/002/build.py`, lines 7–12, 142–147, 245–247]. Step 1 actually completed in Blender 4.2.10 LTS: its log reports 16/16 samples, a saved `final.png` and `scene.blend`, `ok: true`, null error and empty stderr [X0870, `steps/001/result.json`, lines 2–7]. The supplied text contains step 2 code but no step 2 result, and no step 3 code or result for this room; do not infer their individual execution outcomes from the scripts.

**Selection, validation and quality.** `selected.json` records step 3, although the aggregate record’s `selected` field is null [X0867, `selected.json`, lines 1–3; X0866, `record.json`, lines 49–57]. The aggregate record reports completion, three executions on CPU, no runtime error, valid metrics and structural, material and composite passes for target `accessories`. It expressly limits the functional result to structural/edit operability; semantic completeness and support/collision truth are null [X0866, `record.json`, lines 16–38, 47–53, 58–86]. Reported scores include SSIM 0.1670, LPIPS 0.7095 and overall 0.4463 [X0866, lines 17–23]. They are recorded measurements, not independently validated acceptance or a visual judgment. No reference or render image was supplied for inspection, so fidelity and physical connections remain unresolved. The observed step-1 render is a technical success; a completed reconstruction could nevertheless be visually poor.

## Workshop: commons_199974832

**Code intent and representation.** The selected step’s generated script describes an antique woodworking workshop using estimated image coordinates and an arbitrary world scale; it explicitly acknowledges uncertainty about room depth and camera height. It builds semantic `Asset` hierarchies from boxes, rods, balls and swept tubes, with guards for positive dimensions, distinct endpoints and unique child keys. These are code assertions, not collision or fidelity checks. [X0284, `commons_199974832/steps/001/build.py`, lines 8–19, 49–88]

Four shelves and their brackets carry repeated hand planes; each plane groups a body, sole, angled blade and wedge, sometimes handles, before rotation and placement. The bench, vise, saws, chisels, kettle, bottle and nail bowl are similarly assembled from components; the bowl uses 24 radial sides and 65 individually placed nails. Coordinates and common parentage do **not** establish physical contact or image fidelity. [X0284, same file, lines 109–155, 171–194, 267–285, 293–324, 332–372]

**Materials and staging.** The script supplies RGB colors for plaster, wood and iron; apparent grain and printed marks are thin geometry, not image textures. It copies descendant mesh materials and sets Principled BSDF roughness to 0.76 when available; no texture loading is specified. [X0284, same file, lines 36–44, 149–151, 249–265, 409–435] The intended camera is `(0.05, -5.6, 2.85)`, looking at `(0, 1.1, 2.12)` with lens 30. The world strength is 0.65, with three area emitters of powers 750, 550 and 180; the script requests 512 × 384 at 100% and AgX. These are script settings, not independently measured render metadata. [X0284, same file, lines 21–24, 46–47, 383–407, 437–440]

**Execution, selection and validation.** The record reports one execution, CPU rendering, `complete` and `valid: true`. It reports SSIM 0.0844, LPIPS 0.7612, DINO 0.4409, PSNR 8.398 and overall 0.3479. Structural, material and composite checks passed for `Back shelf 0 plane 00`, but the record explicitly limits interpretation to structural/edit operability; semantic completeness and support/collision truth are null. [X0281, `commons_199974832/record.json`, lines 16–47, 58–73] A separate selection file records step 1, although `record.json` says `selected: null`. A later student call timed out after about 450 seconds; the timeout coexists with the completed, valid recorded result, rather than establishing a rendering failure. [X0282, `commons_199974832/selected.json`, lines 1–3; X0281, `commons_199974832/record.json`, lines 49–57]

No reference or rendered image is attached to this audit. The metrics and functional passes are recorded outcomes, not independent visual acceptance: reconstruction quality, detailed contacts and image fidelity cannot be verified here.

### Completed replay: commons_29206714

**Representation and intent.** The generated `steps/003/build.py` assembles a historic kitchen from SceneAct assets and primitives: box-built walls, chimney, hearth and cabinet; separate door panels, hinges and shelves; rod, ball and tube china; a tabletop winder; and a foreground wheel and spindle (X1329, lines 23–117, 119–189, 219–244). The wheel has two swept rims, 12 spokes, hub and supports; doors are rotated −153° and −20° (lines 97–117, 219–235). These are separate parts, not evidence of physically connected or faithful assemblies.

The code hypothesizes that the base was too tall, scales it by 0.82 about the floor, stretches the upper cabinet and contents about the crown, and calculates a winder translation intended to place its feet on the revised counter (X1329, lines 13–15, 191–210). It also describes a preceding nearly edge-on wheel and predicts a more visible ellipse with possible plate occlusion; this is the author’s hypothesis, not a measured visual result (lines 215–218).

**Materials and view.** Colors approximate wood, plaster, china and metal; glass rods specify opacity 0.62. Wear and floral details are geometry, with seeded random variation; the script specifies no image-texture loading or texture mapping (X1329, lines 2–3, 16–19, 48–53, 119–133, 168–175). It enables material nodes and sets available Principled BSDF roughness to 0.62 (lines 259–263). Camera position is `(2.7, −7.5, 2.85)`, target `(0, −0.10, 1.43)`, lens 65; two area emitters and a world background provide lighting (lines 12, 20, 250–258).

**Execution versus validation.** The script asserts positive scale factors and minimum asset-member counts before realization; these are structural checks, not contact or image-quality tests (X1329, lines 246–249). `steps/003/result.json` reports `ok: true`, null error, empty stderr, Blender 4.2.10 LTS, a finished 16/16-sample frame, saved `final.png`, and saved `scene.blend` (X1330, lines 2–7). The task metadata gives a **512×339 reference**, not independently measured output dimensions (X1331, `task.json`, lines 2–5). No measured geometry bounds, image comparison, acceptance score, or actual selection decision for this replay is supplied. The directory name `replay_selection` alone does not establish selection or acceptance. With no images attached, visual fidelity and physical contact remain unverified; successful rendering is not proof of a good reconstruction.

## Modern kitchen selection

The supplied step-3 build describes darkened rear cabinetry, an island, layered sink, extractor, red kettle, posts and exposed ceiling, mostly assembled from boxes with rods, a ball and swept tubes (X0790, `selection/commons_138420790/build.py`, lines 5–11, 37–183). The code matches realized mesh world-space bounds against recorded box centers and sizes to assign explicit finishes, then applies procedural longitudinal noise, ramp and bump to matched posts. That matching is a material-assignment technique, not proof of physical contact (X0790, lines 185–255).

## Historical execution audit

**Scope and provenance.** The supplied record contains step 002’s build and execution result and step 003’s build only. **Step 002 — construction intent.** `002/build.py` creates an antique woodworking-tool room from colored boxes, rods, balls and swept tubes: walls, window, four shelf levels, repeated planes, hanging squares and dividers, a bow saw, and a crowded bench. This is a completed technical render, not a documented rendering failure. The image itself is not supplied, so the log cannot establish appearance or reconstruction quality.

**Step 003 — revised code, execution unobserved.** `003/build.py` retains the primitive-based room and tool collection but darkens its palette, increases the moulding-plane loop from 52 to 56 and side-plane loop from nine to eleven per level, and uses a swept handle for longer planes. This is procedural surface treatment, not an image texture (X0488, lines 224–263).

The revised camera keeps its position but targets `(0,1.18,2.12)` with a 33 mm lens; light powers fall to 430, 240 and 100, and ambient strength to 0.23. It requests 64 Cycles samples, denoising, 512×384, AgX medium-high contrast and exposure −0.3 (X0488, lines 265–283). There is **no step 003 result in the supplied sources**, so neither successful execution nor an error can be inferred for it.

**Selection and acceptance.** No recorded selection, acceptance score, teacher feedback, or visual comparison appears in these supplied files. Thus neither a received teacher judgment nor independently validated quality is established. Even for the successfully rendered step 002, the reconstruction could be visually poor despite technical completion.

## Historical execution audit

**Provenance.** All citations refer to the supplied expanded files under `/srv/lab-disks/disk2/users/xymo/Documents/Codex/2026-11-02/scene-rsi-rsi-1-3d-blender/outputs/scene_rsi_pilot/results/r2/fixed_h0/H0/`. ### Shipboard compartment: development `commons_118588033`

Step 3 code constructs a painted deck and bulkheads, overhead beams and pipes, a three-tier rack of rods, canvas, filleted mattresses and pillows, cabinets, lockers, equipment, a chair turned 12 degrees, and suspended lamps. These are assembled primitive parts, not evidence that every joint physically contacts another part [X0904, `development/commons_118588033/steps/003/build.py`, lines 31–63, 87–107, 111–171, 181–189].

The code assigns principled-material roughness, bright-color emission, and a noise-driven floor bump. It specifies a 27.5 mm camera, five area lights, a dim world, Cycles, denoising, 64 samples and 384 × 512 output [X0904, same file, lines 191–233]. Step 1 failed before rendering: a sink rim fillet radius exceeded half its smallest dimension. Step 2 clamped rounded-box radii but failed because the world was `None`; both results have null images [X0912, `selection/commons_133800864/steps/001/result.json`, lines 2–7; X0913, `selection/commons_133800864/steps/002/build.py`, lines 17–19; X0914, `selection/commons_133800864/steps/002/result.json`, lines 2–7]. The successful code models cabinetry, 43 angled blind slats, a layered pseudo-basin, faucet, separate blender base/cup/control, pitcher and holder parts; the jug is a 64-point-per-ring mesh and the towel two subdivided, solidified grids [X0915, same build file, lines 21–118, 184–237]. `selected.json` records step 3, although the record’s `selected` field is null [X0909, `selection/commons_133800864/selected.json`, lines 1–3; X0908, `selection/commons_133800864/record.json`, lines 53–57]. The record reports valid and functional passes for “blender base,” but explicitly limits these to structural/edit operability; semantic completeness and support/collision truth are null. Its overall score is 0.4241, not an independent visual acceptance [X0908, lines 16–34].

### Industrial kitchen: selection `commons_138420790`

Step 1 failed on a missing world; step 2 added a world and rendered successfully at 16 samples [X0923, `selection/commons_138420790/steps/001/result.json`, lines 2–7; X0924, `selection/commons_138420790/steps/002/build.py`, lines 189–205; X0925, `selection/commons_138420790/steps/002/result.json`, lines 2–7]. A completed render may still be a poor reconstruction; the documented exceptions are technical failures, not visual judgments.

## Historical scene: actual execution audit

**Scope and provenance.** The historical scene is `commons_163096530`. All citations below refer to expanded files under `/srv/lab-disks/disk2/users/xymo/Documents/Codex/2026-11-02/scene-rsi-rsi-1-3d-blender/outputs/scene_rsi_pilot/results/r1/generic_rsi/C2/test/commons_163096530/`. Their archive is `/srv/lab-disks/disk2/users/xymo/Documents/Codex/2026-11-02/scene-rsi-rsi-1-3d-blender/work/scene_archive/bundles/r1--generic_rsi.tar.gz`; the corresponding member is `experiments/scene_rsi_pilot/results/r1/generic_rsi/C2/test/commons_163096530/` plus the cited relative filename. The separate electronics-workbench sources are not evidence for this scene.

### Representation and revisions

The generated code constructs a plaster-and-timber room, individual floorboard boxes, four bunk assemblies, a wardrobe, masonry fireplace, hearth accessories and a clock. Each bunk comprises rod posts and rails, ball caps, box mattresses, covers and pillows, and a rod ladder. The three left bunks are rotated 90 degrees in the later build; a separate wooden panel accompanies the cropped foreground bunk. These are editable primitive assemblies, not evidence of realistic cloth or verified physical joints. [X0597, `build.py`, lines 32–91]

Step 1 used camera `(2.65,-5.5,1.65)`, target `(-.10,3.25,1.48)` and lens 30. Step 2 changed the lens to 37, redirected the target, rotated and repositioned the left bunks, moved the foreground bunk outward and darkened the floor. Its comments attribute these choices to approximate landmark and silhouette comparisons, not measured geometric truth. [X0601, `steps/001/build.py`, lines 6–16, 80–88; X0603, `steps/002/build.py`, lines 5–17, 79–95, 204–210]

The root build describes a further camera-height/pitch correction: camera `(2.65,-5.5,2.15)`, target `(-.42,3.25,1.15)`, lens 37, and foreground x=3.48. Its comments report that the step-2 wardrobe and fireplace appeared too low. These are author estimates and intended corrections, not independent confirmation of the final image. [X0597, `build.py`, lines 5–22]

### Materials and rendering

The root build uses procedural noise, a color ramp and bump for floor grain and selected high fireplace meshes; generated coordinates are multiplied by `(5,.24,3)` for grain. A later material loop sets Principled roughness to `.78`, overriding the texture helper’s `.85` default for applicable materials. No external image texture is specified. [X0597, `build.py`, lines 161–217]

It sets an AgX view, world strength `.40`, and two warm area emitters at powers 650 and 190. The root script itself says it does not render or save; those operations are external to that script. [X0597, `build.py`, lines 161–163, 219–228]

### Execution, validation and acceptance

Steps 1 and 2 each report `ok: true`, null error and empty stderr. Their Blender 4.2.10 logs reach sample 16/16, report finished, and save `final.png` and `scene.blend`; this is successful technical rendering, not a visual-quality verdict. [X0602, `steps/001/result.json`, lines 2–7; X0604, `steps/002/result.json`, lines 2–7]

The record reports three executions, CPU device history, complete status and no runtime error. `selected.json` identifies step 3, while the record’s own `selected` field is null; the supplied evidence contains no step-3 result log, so its individual render cannot be independently checked here. [X0598, `record.json`, lines 16–23, 37–57, 58–86; X0599, `selected.json`, lines 1–3; X0600, `status.json`, lines 1–4]

The record reports validity and an overall score of approximately `.450`, with SSIM `.192`, LPIPS `.718`, DINO `.751`, and PSNR `10.93`. It also reports structure, material, composite and functional passes for `Back narrow bunk`, explicitly limiting their interpretation to structural/edit operability; semantic completeness and support/collision truth are null. Neither these passes nor numeric scores independently establish visual fidelity or physical contact. No reference or rendered image is attached for visual inspection, so a completed but potentially poor reconstruction must not be confused with a technical rendering failure. [X0598, `record.json`, lines 17–34]

**Code intent and representation.** The selected step’s generated script builds a compartment from individually included, serially named boxes, rods, filleted boxes and flattened balls—not a single semantic mesh. The script assigns colors to primitives, sets node-based Principled materials to roughness 0.69 and conditionally gives very bright materials emission strength 3. **View and render intent.** The camera is placed at (-0.05, -0.90, 1.67), aimed at (-0.20, 6, 1.69), with 25.5 mm lens and 36 mm sensor. The script creates a world if absent, sets background strength 0.35, and requests Cycles, 48 samples, 384 × 512 at 100%, AgX and medium-high contrast. Step 2 reduced that radius to 0.075 but failed at `sc.world.color` because the world was `None`; its image was also null. Step 3 explicitly creates a missing world. [X0422, `commons_118588033/steps/001/build.py`, lines 125–126; X0423, `commons_118588033/steps/001/result.json`, lines 2–7; X0424, `commons_118588033/steps/002/build.py`, lines 138–141, 219–220; X0425, `commons_118588033/steps/002/result.json`, lines 2–7; X0426, lines 292–298]

The run record reports completion, no runtime error, CPU execution and three executions; a separate selection file identifies step 3, although the record’s own `selected` field is null. The supplied sources do not include step 3’s result log, so the record supports reported completion but does not independently show its render-save log. [X0419, `commons_118588033/record.json`, lines 16–18, 36–38, 47–57; X0420, `commons_118588033/selected.json`, lines 1–3; X0421, `commons_118588033/status.json`, lines 1–4]

**Validation and quality.** The record reports valid=true, SSIM 0.1339, LPIPS 0.8928, DINO 0.6055, PSNR 10.7852 and overall 0.3480. Its structural, material and composite checks pass for `bunk pillow_0025`, but the record explicitly limits interpretation to structural/edit operability; semantic completeness and support-collision truth are null. These results are not independent visual acceptance or proof of contact. No images are attached for visual judgment; a completed reconstruction could still be poor. [X0419, lines 17–35]

Blender 4.2.10 reported success, empty stderr, 16/16 samples, saved PNG and blend [X0244, `selection/commons_133800864/steps/003/result.json`, lines 2–7]. No selection or acceptance record for this scene is supplied.

## Showroom: commons_138420790

The generated scene uses editable named composites for the shell, timber, rear cabinets, ovens and surround, island, sink, hob, extractor and kettle. The step 3 code describes a revision in response to its own step 2 observations: closer framing, a deeper wall, a wider chaise and reduced lighting and floor reflectance. The scene uses colored boxes, filleted boxes, rods, swept tubes and balls: parquet and paneling, glazing and curtain folds, a carved chaise with legs, frame, mattress, headrest and ornament, a separate pillow and label, and a cane-strand foreground chair. This establishes successful recorded rendering, not visual fidelity. No selection or acceptance record for this scene is supplied.

## Historical Scene execution audit

**Provenance.** The supplied records are expanded under `/srv/lab-disks/disk2/users/xymo/Documents/Codex/2026-11-02/scene-rsi-rsi-1-3d-blender/outputs/scene_rsi_pilot/results/replay_s1_r1/M_initial/C1/replay_selection/commons_29206714/`. Their corresponding archive is `/srv/lab-disks/disk2/users/xymo/Documents/Codex/2026-11-02/scene-rsi-rsi-1-3d-blender/work/scene_archive/bundles/replay_s1_r1--M_initial.tar.gz`; each member is under `experiments/scene_rsi_pilot/results/replay_s1_r1/M_initial/C1/replay_selection/commons_29206714/`, followed by the same relative filename. Citations below give source ID, expanded relative filename, and exact lines.

**Generated representation.** Both builds use `sceneact` assets assembled from boxes, rods, balls and swept tubes. The architecture includes a floor, walls, chimney, dark hearth, individual stones and flattened random plaster-wear balls (X1325, `steps/001/build.py`, lines 22–54). The cabinet is a multipart construction with drawer, paneled base doors, shelves, crown and separately pivoted upper doors at −153° and −20° (X1325, lines 56–114). Plates are thin rods with tube rims and ball floral details; vessels combine scaled balls, rods and optional tube handles, with separate spouts. Glasses, bottles and pewter-colored vessels are also included (X1325, lines 116–166). The tabletop winder, foreground wheel and separate spindle are likewise composites; the wheel has two tube rims, 12 spokes, hub and supports (X1325, lines 168–203).

**Materials and texture.** Colors are supplied as numeric finishes, including wood, china and metal-colored surfaces; glass rods request opacity 0.62. The script globally sets available Principled BSDF roughness to 0.62. Wear and plate decoration are modeled geometry, not evidenced image textures; the complete build contains no explicit texture-image loading or mapping operation (X1325, lines 15–18, 48–53, 116–128, 158–160, 215–220).

**Pose and framing intent.** Step 1 sets the camera at (2.7, −7.5, 2.85), targeting (0, −0.10, 1.43), lens 52; its wheel center is (−1.05, −1.02, 0.78), radius 0.76, turned 67° (X1325, lines 12–14, 181–195). Step 2 calls for lens 65 to tighten framing, but also changes the wheel center to (−0.65, −1.02, 0.55), radius to 1.05 and angle to −62°, with lower support coordinates. Thus the camera comment’s “focal length only” describes the camera adjustment, not the whole revision. Its predicted narrow, cropped wheel and changed occlusion remain hypotheses, not measured image outcomes (X1327, `steps/002/build.py`, lines 12–18, 195–217).

Both scripts specify a warm area light of power 650 and size 3.2, a cooler fill of power 130 and size 2.5, and world background strength 0.32 (X1325, lines 19, 205–214; X1327, lines 230–239). The logs record 16/16 samples; resolution is not reported in the supplied text (X1326, `steps/001/result.json`, line 5; X1328, `steps/002/result.json`, line 5).

**Execution and validation.** Both results report `ok: true`, null errors and empty stderr. Blender 4.2.10 LTS logs finish frame 1, save `final.png` and `scene.blend`, and report total times of 5.83 and 6.95 seconds respectively (X1326, lines 2–7; X1328, lines 2–7). No technical rendering failure is recorded. Step 2 adds finite-positive dimension and asset-member-count assertions before realization; successful completion indicates these did not stop that run, but they do not test contact, composition or fidelity (X1327, lines 227–230; X1328, lines 2–6).

**Selection and quality limits.** The directory is named `replay_selection`, but these records contain no explicit selection decision, acceptance score or independent quality assessment. No reference or rendered image is attached for inspection. The anchor comments and coordinate choices are intent, not measured image geometry; coordinates alone cannot establish contact among wheel parts, vessels, shelves or other assemblies. A completed render may still be a poor reconstruction, and visual fidelity and actual cropping remain unverified.

A different selection scene, `commons_138420790`, logged a successful step-3 16-sample render; its code and selection decision are not in this source set. [X0927, `selection/commons_138420790/steps/003/result.json`, lines 2–7]

**Conclusion:** Successful rendering and operability checks are not independent visual-quality acceptance. The reported similarity scores, absent images, and null contact/completeness fields leave reconstruction quality and physical connections unresolved; timeouts and exceptions are technical failures, distinct from completed renders that may still reconstruct poorly.

**Conclusion:** Recorded render completion and operability are distinct from reconstruction quality. Neither coordinates nor ownership establish physical contact, and the supplied text cannot independently verify image fidelity.

# Historical Scene execution audit

**Scope and provenance.** Paths below share the expanded-file root `/srv/lab-disks/disk2/users/xymo/Documents/Codex/2026-11-02/scene-rsi-rsi-1-3d-blender/outputs/scene_rsi_pilot/results/r1/` and archive root `/srv/lab-disks/disk2/users/xymo/Documents/Codex/2026-11-02/scene-rsi-rsi-1-3d-blender/work/scene_archive/bundles/`. Archive member paths are `experiments/scene_rsi_pilot/results/r1/` followed by the expanded-file suffix cited below. The fixed-H0 test and generic-RSI development records concern **different scenes** and must not be combined as one execution.

## Generic-RSI development: chaise reconstruction

**Code intent and representation.** `generic_rsi/C1/development/commons_109923183/steps/001/build.py` builds a paneled room and staggered diagonal parquet from boxes; the chaise is a separate assembly of swept rails, curved legs, arms, ornament and rounded foot frame. A separate cane-chair assembly uses rims and a woven grid; rods represent curtains. Separately created meshes form cushions, side upholstery, pillow and a small seat label [X0495, lines 35–73, 75–159, 205–260]. These are modeled components, not evidence that all parts physically connect.

The later top-level `generic_rsi/C1/development/commons_109923183/build.py` records a revision intended to enlarge the chaise in frame, straighten the wall and darken the scene. It moves the camera from `(2.65,-6.3,4.0)` to `(.30,-4.0,3.20)`, retains a 48 mm lens and rotates the chaise −12° independently of the architecture [X0495, lines 5–13; X0491, lines 5–20, 266–280]. The code creates the seat and back as smooth parametric meshes, a solidified and beveled side panel, and a rotated loose pillow [X0491, lines 210–250].

**Materials and rendering intent.** The later code uses colored primitives and node-based upholstery: generated Y coordinates pass through multiplication by 145, sine and a color ramp for stripes; noise at scale 170 drives bump. Pillow noise drives a separate bump [X0491, lines 167–204]. A blue-gray world at strength 0.19, a 420-power warm area light, a 45-power fill and AgX are specified; the runner, not this script, manages render and save [X0491, lines 282–295]. These settings are code evidence, not a visual assessment.

**Execution and validation.** The run record reports completion, three executions, CPU, no runtime error and valid metrics: SSIM 0.2513, LPIPS 0.7548, DINO 0.6103, PSNR 13.6936 and overall 0.4339. Its functional checks pass for `Architecture`, but explicitly measure structural/edit operability; semantic completeness and support/collision truth are null [X0492, lines 16–38, 47–53]. The separate selection file records **step 2**, whereas the record’s `selected` field is null [X0493, lines 1–3; X0492, lines 56–57]. The supplied selection does not establish which code revision was rendered or independently validate visual quality. No image is attached for inspection.

## Fixed-H0 test: separate execution

For `fixed_h0/H0/test/commons_199974832`, task metadata names a 512 × 384 reference image; it does not establish the output image dimensions [X0490, lines 2–5]. The step-3 result reports `ok: true`, `error: null`, empty stderr, and Blender 4.2.10 LTS synchronizing numerous objects named Box, Rod and Ball. Its log reaches sample 16/16, reports the frame finished, saves `final.png` and saves `scene.blend` [X0489, lines 2–7]. This is evidence of a completed render, not a technical rendering failure, but the log alone does not identify the represented subject, construction code, materials, camera or visual fidelity. No selection or acceptance for this test is supplied.

**Conclusion.** The recorded render completion and development-run functional passes are narrower than reconstruction quality. With no rendered or reference images attached, neither fidelity nor part contact can be independently judged; the numeric metrics and recorded selection are not independent visual acceptance.

## Kitchen corner: commons_133800864

**Generated representation and revisions.** Step 1 built semantic assemblies from boxes, rods, balls and swept tubes, including a cabinet, blinds, paper roll, faucet, blender, jug and pitcher. The selected step 3 code deepened the cabinet to 0.72, extended the counter, subtracted a sink opening and modeled a separate recessed floor and side walls. It used 40 blind slats, a 40-by-28 towel mesh with solidify and subdivision modifiers, and separate jug, pitcher and hardware parts (X0155, `R/commons_133800864/steps/003/build.py`, lines 4–15, 32–75, 96–153, 218–249). **Execution and selection.** All three result files report `ok: true`, null error, empty stderr, a finished 16-sample render, saved PNG and blend file; the logged 16 samples differ from the 48 specified in the scripts. The source does not explain why (X0152, `R/commons_133800864/steps/001/result.json`, lines 2–7; X0154, `R/commons_133800864/steps/002/result.json`, lines 2–7; X0156, `R/commons_133800864/steps/003/result.json`, lines 2–7). `selected.json` records step 3, while the record's separate `selected` field is null (X0149, `R/commons_133800864/selected.json`, line 2; X0148, `R/commons_133800864/record.json`, line 57).

## Contemporary kitchen: commons_138420790

No reference or rendered images were supplied for visual inspection. Neither coordinates nor grouping establish physical contact, support, or image fidelity.

### Smoke 01: completed kitchen, not verified fidelity

The selected step-3 code constructs a corner kitchen from separately included boxes, rods and a swept tube: cabinet carcasses, doors and pulls; two window assemblies; and a countertop with subtracted openings, a compressed rounded sink rim, two subtracted bowls, drains and faucet parts. These are **code representations and intended relationships**, not proof that the parts physically contact or match the photograph [X1470, `smoke_01/steps/003/build.py`, lines 19–57, 74–149].

After realization, the code assigns procedural noise-and-ramp timber using generated coordinates, metallic chrome, emissive window surfaces and a noise-and-ramp countertop; it does not show image-texture loading. It specifies camera `(3.05,-4.05,2.20)` toward `(.85,-.75,1.35)`, 44 mm lens, three area lights, a world background, 256×171 Cycles and 48 samples [X1470, lines 151–241]. **Actual render metadata differs:** the successful Blender log reports 16/16 samples, saves `final.png` and `scene.blend`, and returns `ok: true`; the supplied evidence does not establish why the sample count changed [X1471, `smoke_01/steps/003/result.json`, lines 2–7].

Step 1 failed because its sink-rim fillet exceeded half the smallest dimension; step 2 corrected that construction but failed when the world was `None`. Step 3 creates a world if necessary [X1467, `smoke_01/steps/001/result.json`, lines 2–7; X1469, `smoke_01/steps/002/result.json`, lines 2–7; X1470, lines 222–231]. Step 3 was selected [X1464, `smoke_01/selected.json`, line 2]. The non-formal record reports valid output, SSIM 0.285 and overall 0.528; material and composite checks passed, but structure and functional checks failed for the curved faucet. Semantic completeness and support/collision truth were not measured [X1463, `smoke_01/record.json`, lines 14–25, 36–45]. No image was supplied for visual inspection, so completion and these metrics do not establish visual fidelity.

### Iterative chaise reconstruction

The generated code represents a chaise as a grouped asset of filleted-box upholstery and apron, segmented rod legs and frame, ball ornaments, and a separately rotated pillow; architecture and a cropped cane chair are other assets. The last build declares a 2.52 × 1.03 × 0.30 seat and a 0.65 × 0.27 × 0.67 pillow, not measured rendered geometry (X1129, `sceneevolve_cli/C1/development/commons_109923183/steps/003/build.py`, lines 18–20, 22–23, 51–112).

The code replaces matching material slots with procedural sine-dash/noise-bump textile, rotated brick parquet, and checker cane. Its camera is `(0.55,-5.35,3.25)` aimed at `(0.045,0.04,0.93)`, lens 56, with two area lights, ambient strength 0.25 and 512 × 384 AgX output (X1129, lines 116–224). The task lists a 512 × 384 reference; that is reference metadata, not visual confirmation (X1131, same scene’s `task.json`, lines 2–5).

The initial code comments give image-anchor estimates; subsequent comments diagnose size, camera, pillow and floor issues. Those are author observations, not independently verified image measurements (X1125, same scene’s `steps/001/build.py`, lines 1–6; X1127, `steps/002/build.py`, lines 1–3; X1129, lines 1–3). Steps 1–3 each report successful Blender 4.2.10 renders, 16/16 samples, saved PNG and blend, and empty stderr (X1126, `steps/001/result.json`, lines 2–7; X1128, `steps/002/result.json`, lines 2–7; X1130, `steps/003/result.json`, lines 2–7). `selected.json` records step 3, while the scene record’s `selected` field is null; these are distinct selection fields (X1123, `selected.json`, line 2; X1122, `record.json`, lines 56–57).

### Baselines and validation limits

The metalworking workshop code uses individual boards, nails, a bench, anvil, swept flywheels, vises and rod-built bucket sides; its noise/ramp/bump materials and three lights are explicit (X1112, `scene_rsi_pilot/H0/test/commons_155212263/build.py`, lines 26–38, 62–78, 95–155, 212–229, 248–302). ## Generated scene and execution

The completed development scene’s selected build represents a French sitting room: boxed architecture and individual herringbone parquet boards; an antique chaise assembled from a filleted seat and underframe, swept apron, four swept legs and feet, head and arm frames; separate upholstered panels, pillow, card, partial cane chair and curtains. The pillow is rotated about two axes and the chair about Z. These are code-defined parts and placements, not proof of physical contact or faithful appearance. [X0125, `results/ablation_r1/generic_same_structured_evidence/C1/development/commons_109923183/build.py`, `ablation_r1--generic_same_structured_evidence.tar.gz`, lines 27–51, 91–154, 174–212, 286–307]

The build assigns procedural linen: high-frequency noise bump, with thresholded X/Y wave bands mixed into rose-colored dashes for patterned upholstery; the pillow gets plain linen. Parquet meshes receive randomly chosen walnut materials using scaled generated coordinates, noise and bump. These are shader operations in code, not a measured guarantee that grain or motifs are visible. [X0125, same file and archive, lines 228–284, 313–340]

The intended camera is at `(1.22, -4.90, 3.65)`, aimed at `(.12, .08, .96)`, 48 mm lens and −5° roll. The code sets a 0.20-strength world, warm 390-power and cool 65-power area emitters, 512×384 PNG, Cycles, 48 samples, denoising, AgX and −0.25 exposure. These are build settings, not independently measured final-render metadata. [X0125, same file and archive, lines 12–20, 342–366]

The record reports **complete**, valid, three executions, no runtime error and CPU render history. Its recorded image metrics are SSIM 0.2951, LPIPS 0.7617, DINO 0.7140, PSNR 15.03 and overall 0.46345. Structure, material and composite operability pass, but semantic completeness and support/collision truth are explicitly null; the tested target is `apron_fine_line_0`. Thus these passes do not validate the whole chaise or its contacts. [X0126, `results/ablation_r1/generic_same_structured_evidence/C1/development/commons_109923183/record.json`, same archive, lines 16–34, 37–53, 65–79] The recorded selected step is **2**, not a quality certification. [X0127, same directory’s `selected.json`, same archive, lines 1–3]

## Failures, selection and visual assessment

A retired H0 test illustrates a **pre-execution service failure**, not a bad Blender render: the student exited 1, classified `cli_usage_limit`; executions were zero, `scene.blend` or `final.png` was missing, metrics were null and functional checks failed. [X0031, `quota_restored_20261103/retired/results/r1/fixed_h0/H0/test/commons_155212263/record.json`, `history--quota_restored_20261103.tar.gz`, lines 16–31, 36–50] The corresponding pilot record marks H0 selected while reusing that failed result; selection therefore did not establish acceptance or quality. [X0065, `quota_restored_20261103/retired/results/r1/scene_rsi_pilot/H0/test/commons_155212263/record.json`, same archive, lines 16–24, 35–50, 60–70] A separate C1 proposal was rejected without execution for a disallowed execution primitive. [X0002, `candidates/r2/scene_rsi_pilot/C1/rejected_proposal/status.json`, `r2--scene_rsi_pilot.tar.gz`, lines 2–5]

The completed anonymous classroom judgment reports an **adapted, not official**, fused score of 54.72. It describes recognizable furniture and layout but heavy noise, washed-out surfaces and simplified detail; it could not establish grain, exact finished raster, saved camera optics, original Cycles settings or effective denoising. It cautions that AABB candidates do not establish mesh collisions. This is a potentially poor visual reconstruction, distinct from the failed runs above; the score is not independently validated acceptance. [X0006, `external_judgments/anon_02/result.json`, `shared-evidence.tar.gz`, lines 2–6, 29–51, 55–78, 86–93] Other external judgments were not run after reconstruction failure or failed themselves. ## Execution audit

**Scope and provenance.** The principal case is `commons_109923183`, development candidate C1, method `ours_without_regression_guidance`. Its expanded files are under `/srv/lab-disks/disk2/users/xymo/Documents/Codex/2026-11-02/scene-rsi-rsi-1-3d-blender/outputs/scene_rsi_pilot/results/ablation_r1/ours_without_regression_guidance/C1/development/commons_109923183/`. Citations below identify the source, file suffix, and original numbered lines.

**Generated representation and intent.** Step 1 describes an elevated view of an ivory-striped chaise, green and gilt frame, curved feet, pillow and label, against paneling, curtain and parquet, with a cropped cane chair. It assigns the chaise’s long axis to world X and treats scale as arbitrary [X0291, `steps/001/build.py`, lines 8–12]. Its code builds separate semantic assets and primitive or swept-tube components: a seat support, apron, four legs and feet, crest and rear rails, rosettes and scrolls; a curved head shell and rear wing are separate meshes, with a superellipsoid-like mattress and rotated pillow [X0291, lines 84–136, 204–265]. These are intended relationships, not demonstrated physical contacts.

Step 2 retains the composition but batches parquet and cane into meshes explicitly to bound realization cost, replacing step 1’s individual plank and cane objects [X0293, `steps/002/build.py`, lines 138–155, 181–225, 363–378; X0291, lines 58–82, 147–159]. Its parquet uses eight shared wood finishes and noise-driven bump; upholstery uses generated-coordinate sine thresholding for rose stripes plus noise bump, and the pillow has a separate noise bump [X0293, lines 181–200, 227–268]. The code specifies a 2.49 × 1.00 × 0.15 support, mattress radii 1.28 × 0.515 × 0.155, and pillow rotation (0, −18, −7) degrees; these are code parameters, not measured contact or image fidelity [X0293, lines 69–72, 273–294, 320–321].

**Camera and render changes.** Steps 1 and 2 use camera (2.6, −5.0, 3.8), target (0, 0, 0.95), 61 mm lens and 36 mm sensor, two area emitters, Cycles and 512 × 384 output. Step 1 specifies 48 samples; step 2 specifies 24 and denoising [X0291, lines 312–329; X0293, lines 380–399]. The root `build.py` proposes a more frontal camera at (1.05, −5.8, 3.90), target (0.25, 0, 0.95), 58 mm lens, zero shift, while retaining the lighting and 24-sample setup. Its comments attribute excessive diagonals to camera displacement: that is the author’s hypothesis, not independent visual validation [X0287, `build.py`, lines 8–16, 370–393]. The supplied text does not establish that this root file is identical to the selected step’s executed code.

**Execution, selection and validation.** Step 1 actually timed out after approximately 600 seconds; `ok` is false, stdout and stderr are empty, and image is null. This is a technical execution failure, not evidence of a bad-looking completed render [X0292, `steps/001/result.json`, lines 2–7]. No step-2 or step-3 execution result is supplied. The separate selection file records step 3, while the run record’s `selected` field is null [X0289, `selected.json`, line 2; X0288, `record.json`, line 57]. The record nevertheless reports complete, valid, overall 0.4433, SSIM 0.2643, LPIPS 0.7523 and DINO 0.6358; it reports structural, material and composite passes, but explicitly limits interpretation to edit operability and leaves support/collision truth and semantic completeness null [X0288, lines 16–34]. These reported scores and passes do not independently establish visual quality or contact. No acceptance decision or teacher-policy receipt is shown.

**Separate test execution.** For `commons_199974832` under `ours_without_probes/C1/test`, expanded `steps/001/result.json` is X0285; its archive is `/srv/lab-disks/disk2/users/xymo/Documents/Codex/2026-11-02/scene-rsi-rsi-1-3d-blender/work/scene_archive/bundles/ablation_r1--ours_without_probes.tar.gz`, member `experiments/scene_rsi_pilot/results/ablation_r1/ours_without_probes/C1/test/commons_199974832/steps/001/result.json`. Blender 4.2.10 reports frame 1 finished at 16/16 samples, saves `final.png` and `scene.blend`, with `ok: true`, null error and empty stderr [X0285, lines 2–6]. This establishes a completed render, not its visual quality; its generated code, selection and acceptance are not supplied. The task metadata specifies a 512 × 384 reference [X0286, expanded `task.json`, lines 2–5; same archive, corresponding `experiments/.../commons_199974832/task.json` member]. No images were provided for visual assessment.

## Historical scene execution audit

**Provenance and scope.** The historical metalworking shop is test scene `commons_155212263`, not the supplied kitchen selection scenes. Citations below use source IDs and original line numbers. All cited files are under the expanded directory `/srv/lab-disks/disk2/users/xymo/Documents/Codex/2026-11-02/scene-rsi-rsi-1-3d-blender/outputs/scene_rsi_pilot/results/r2/sceneevolve_cli/C1/test/commons_155212263/`. Their corresponding archive is `/srv/lab-disks/disk2/users/xymo/Documents/Codex/2026-11-02/scene-rsi-rsi-1-3d-blender/work/scene_archive/bundles/r2--sceneevolve_cli.tar.gz`; each member path is `experiments/scene_rsi_pilot/results/r2/sceneevolve_cli/C1/test/commons_155212263/` followed by the cited relative filename.

**Generated representation and intent.** The selected step-2 script constructs a static shop from named `sceneact` assets and primitive boxes, rods, and balls. It uses 32 randomly varied floor planks, a windowed plaster wall, and a 9.4 × 1.06 × 0.19 coded workbench slab with legs, feet, stretchers and scars. Three lathes share a component-generating function; each has bedrails, feet, pedestals, spindle, headstock, chuck, tailstock, rest and crank. The far lathe, the functional target, is instantiated at x = −4.20, length 1.2, scale 0.7. These are **script parameters**, not independently measured geometry (X1171, `steps/002/build.py`, lines 39–105).

The drive wheel is assembled from 28 rod segments, bent two-segment spokes and a hub; other assets include a stepped anvil and horn, projecting vise, open drawer, loose tools and bins. The code groups parts but does not establish physical contact, mechanical operation or image fidelity; no collision or support measurement is supplied (X1171, lines 23–37, 107–187; X1166, `record.json`, lines 25–34).

**Materials and view.** Step 2 darkens the iron and timber in response to comments that the first view was distant and pale; those comments are design feedback, not independently verified observations. Node operations set general roughness to 0.85, conditionally assign metallic 0.60, add noise-driven bump and color ramps, and give bright materials emission (X1171, lines 5–15, 211–240). The coded camera is at (4.6, −6.3, 3.7), aimed at (−0.65, 0.30, 1.04), with a 46 mm lens and 36 mm sensor; two area lights have powers 550 and 130. The script requests Cycles, 32 samples, denoising, AgX and 512 × 384 at 100% (X1171, lines 242–259).

**Execution and selection.** Step 1 actually completed in Blender 4.2.10, with `ok: true`, null error, empty stderr, a saved PNG and blend file; its log reports 16/16 samples, despite its script requesting 32. This discrepancy cautions against treating script settings as measured render settings (X1169, `steps/001/build.py`, lines 224–240; X1170, `steps/001/result.json`, lines 2–7). The supplied text does **not** include the step-2 or step-3 execution result, so their individual render completion cannot be independently confirmed here. The overall record nevertheless reports completion, three executions, CPU rendering and no runtime error; `selected.json` explicitly selects **step 2**, while the record’s `selected` field is null (X1166, lines 16–23, 36–57, 65–86; X1167, `selected.json`, lines 1–3). The top-level `build.py` has the same supplied text as step 2 (X1165, `build.py`, lines 1–7, 242–259; X1171, lines 1–7, 242–259).

**Validation and quality.** The record reports valid=true and structural, material, composite and functional passes, explicitly limited to structural/edit operability; semantic completeness and support/collision truth are null. Recorded metrics are SSIM 0.163, LPIPS 0.656, DINO 0.718, PSNR 11.49 and overall 0.455 (X1166, lines 17–34). These are recorded measurements, not independent acceptance of visual quality. No reference or rendered images were supplied for visual inspection. Thus this is a recorded completed reconstruction, potentially a poor match, not evidence of a technical rendering failure or of verified visual fidelity.
