# Historical execution audit

**Provenance.** Expanded files share prefix `/srv/lab-disks/disk2/users/xymo/Documents/Codex/2026-11-02/scene-rsi-rsi-1-3d-blender/outputs/scene_rsi_pilot/results/r2/`. References below give the remaining file path, source ID and exact lines. Their archived counterparts are in `/srv/lab-disks/disk2/users/xymo/Documents/Codex/2026-11-02/scene-rsi-rsi-1-3d-blender/work/scene_archive/bundles/`: `r2--generic_rsi.tar.gz` or `r2--scene_rsi_pilot.tar.gz`, respectively, under member prefix `experiments/scene_rsi_pilot/results/r2/` followed by the same remaining path.

## Kitchen corner: C2 selection

The scripts build semantic assemblies from boxes, rods, balls and swept tubes: blinds, framed cabinet, roll and ring, panel-based towel, sink, faucet and appliances. Step 3 subtracts a sink opening and independently moves the pitcher and jug; these are construction operations, not proof of contact or occlusion fidelity (X1045, `generic_rsi/C2/selection/commons_133800864/steps/003/build.py`, lines 77–155, 157–189, 240–285). The step-2 and step-3 ledgers describe cropping, stiff towel folds and a previously hidden basin as **author observations and revision hypotheses**, not independently verified measurements (X1043, `.../steps/002/build.py`, lines 4–14; X1045, lines 4–15).

Step 3 uses object-referenced noise, a color ramp and bump for granite; copied materials add fine bump to paper and cloth, metallic settings to ring, faucet and sink, and separate plastic roughness (X1045, lines 312–389). Its camera is `(-2.50,-2.15,2.405)`, target `(.10,.38,1.525)`, 66 mm; ambient strength is .17, with 145 and 48 power area lights and AgX (X1045, lines 34–39, 391–402).

All three results report `ok: true`, null error, empty stderr, 16/16 samples, saved PNG and blend. Printed scene-solid counts are 274, 266 and 261, respectively; these are hierarchy counts, not visual validation (X1042, `.../steps/001/result.json`, lines 2–7; X1044, `.../steps/002/result.json`, lines 2–7; X1046, `.../steps/003/result.json`, lines 2–7). The reference metadata says 384×512, not necessarily the rendered resolution (X1047, `.../task.json`, lines 2–5). No selection or acceptance record for this scene is supplied.

## Island kitchen: C2 selection

The final code uses a block-built island and hood, layered sink, swept curved tap, ceiling beams, timber, rear cabinets, kettle and paired ovens. The sink is represented by thin layered boxes rather than a subtracted bowl (X1056, `generic_rsi/C2/selection/commons_138420790/steps/003/build.py`, lines 58–117, 126–165). It applies shared-coordinate noise/ramp/bump wood, brick-pattern floor color and a noise-bumped hood material (X1056, lines 212–279). Camera `(-5.5,-3.8,2.48)` targets `(0,.55,1.48)` at 47 mm; four area emitters and AgX are specified (X1056, lines 35–37, 287–302).

The revision ledger attributes step 2’s dark foreground to the camera being outside the left wall; moving that wall is a code response, not independent proof of the final image’s quality (X1056, lines 4–11, 52–55). All three step results report success, empty stderr, 16/16 samples and saved outputs; printed solid counts rise from 103 to 103 to 115 (X1053, `.../steps/001/result.json`, lines 2–7; X1055, `.../steps/002/result.json`, lines 2–7; X1057, `.../steps/003/result.json`, lines 2–7). `selected.json` records step 3, while the record’s separate `selected` field is null (X1050, `.../selected.json`, lines 1–3; X1049, `.../record.json`, lines 53–57). The record reports overall .440 and functional passes, but explicitly limits those passes to structural/edit operability; completeness and support/collision truth are null (X1049, lines 16–34).

## Execution audit

**Provenance.** Let `P` denote `/srv/lab-disks/disk2/users/xymo/Documents/Codex/2026-11-02/scene-rsi-rsi-1-3d-blender/`. ### Dormitory — fixed H0, commons_163096530

Step 1 failed before producing an image: `world.use_nodes` raised an AttributeError because the world was `None` (X0958, `fixed_h0/H0/test/commons_163096530/steps/001/result.json`, lines 2–7). Step 2 explicitly creates a world if absent. Its generated scene uses individual boards and grain strips, plaster and timbers, three left bunk assemblies with separate posts, rails, mattresses, sheets and ladders, a wardrobe, fireplace, guard and foreground bunk (X0959, `fixed_h0/H0/test/commons_163096530/steps/002/build.py`, lines 16–101, 103–180, 195–224).

The code sets roughness to .79 and attempts noise-driven bump on materials encountered on named surfaces; it tracks processed material names, so assignment depends on encounter order. It specifies a 31.5 lens, two area emitters, ambient world, Cycles and 512×341 PNG, with 48 samples (X0959, lines 226–275). Actual step 2 succeeded and saved PNG and blend files, but the render log reports **16/16 samples**, not the scripted 48 (X0960, `fixed_h0/H0/test/commons_163096530/steps/002/result.json`, lines 2–7). The reference metadata is 512×341 (X0963, `fixed_h0/H0/test/commons_163096530/task.json`, lines 2–5); that is not an independently measured output size.

Step 3 changed the floor, bunk positions and foreground crop, added ceiling flakes and color-conditioned metallic or procedural grain/bump materials, and specified lens 39 and 64 samples with denoising (X0961, `fixed_h0/H0/test/commons_163096530/steps/003/build.py`, lines 16–52, 54–98, 173–203, 207–271). It timed out after about 464 seconds with no reported image; its intended changes cannot be treated as a completed render (X0962, `fixed_h0/H0/test/commons_163096530/steps/003/result.json`, lines 2–7). No selection or acceptance record for this scene was supplied.

### Woodworking room — fixed H0, commons_199974832

The generated design represents shelves of many separate hand planes, chisels and hanging tools, plus a crowded workbench with saws, containers and screw trays. Planes use separate bodies, soles, inclined blades, wedges and swept handles; instances are rotated and translated (X0968, `fixed_h0/H0/test/commons_199974832/steps/002/build.py`, lines 34–120, 129–165, 167–257). Its wood-like colors receive mapped noise, a color ramp and bump; near-neutral dark colors receive metallic shading. The code specifies a 31 mm lens, three area lights, a guarded ambient world, Cycles, 48 samples and 512×384 output (X0968, lines 261–317; reference dimensions: X0970, `fixed_h0/H0/test/commons_199974832/task.json`, lines 2–5).

Step 2 added world creation but timed out after about 425 seconds, without an image (X0969, `fixed_h0/H0/test/commons_199974832/steps/002/result.json`, lines 2–7). This is a technical failure, not evidence that a completed reconstruction looked poor.

### Chaise — generic RSI, commons_109923183

The final expanded build describes a separate cushioned seat, resized and rotated padded headboard, swept frame, arms, aprons and legs, pillow, label and cropped cane chair. ## Scope and outcome

This audit uses only the supplied B11 records, not the entire archive. Archive: `/srv/lab-disks/disk2/users/xymo/Documents/Codex/2026-11-02/scene-rsi-rsi-1-3d-blender/work/scene_archive/bundles/history--quota_restored_20261103.tar.gz`. Member paths below are relative to `experiments/scene_rsi_pilot/quota_restored_20261103/proposal_context_before_retry/candidates/`.

**The selected proposal attempts failed technically; the records do not establish candidate acceptance or an improvement in student performance.** Each supplied C2 `proposal_result.json` reports `proposal_failed`, proposer exit 1 and `M_changed: false`; the six ablation C1/C2 results report the same. The underlying stderr logs are not supplied, so the cause of exit is unknown. Existing C2 policy files can be inherited parent code, not evidence that these failed proposals produced updates. For example, `r1/scene_rsi_pilot/C2/proposal_result.json` keys `status`, `error`, `parent_source`, `M_changed` [0,324); `r2/generic_rsi/C2/proposal_result.json` [0,312).

## Detection and controlled evidence

- The initial teacher distinguished missing output from visual disagreement. In `r2/scene_rsi_pilot/C2/diagnostic_observations.json`, `initial_diagnoses` [0,12287), `commons_109923183` has no valid output and unknown cause; for `commons_118588033`, camera framing or scale is explicitly a hypothesis, not attribution. Its completed focal probe changed 27.5 to 30.25 with geometry unchanged: overall 0.39655 to 0.39264. Scaling the selected `bunks` group by 1.1 at fixed camera yielded 0.39504. These are scoped sensitivities, not proof of the correct camera or 3D size.
- `r1/scene_rsi_pilot/C2/diagnostic_observations.json`, `initial_diagnoses` [0,12942), records failed execution events at steps 1 and 3, but both stderr strings are empty. Pre-execution API review is a proposed remedy, not an established failure cause. A separate missing-item explanation—omission, overwrite, crop, occlusion or low contrast—is hypothetical; isolation was not run.
- In `ablation_r1/generic_same_structured_evidence/C1/diagnostic_observations.json`, `probes` [0,4078), focal 51→56.1 changes overall 0.39075→0.38753, while 1.1 scaling of `arm gold-1` gives 0.39096. Selection is a lexicographic group rule, not proof of semantic whole-object coverage. The no-probes ablation instead has `probes: []`; the no-regression-guidance ablation withholds feedback while stating that final gates remain fixed (`ablation_r1/ours_without_probes/C1/meta_execution.json` [0,2000); `ablation_r1/ours_without_regression_guidance/C1/meta_execution.json` [0,2102)).

## Coverage, materials and connections

The r1 diagnostics report prescribed Blender material and move operations on `apron_-1` and `architecture`: target material replacement and non-target preservation passed; moves preserved selected part-relative matrices within tolerance, with three renders completed for each. These are **edit-operability tests**, not proof of material or texture fidelity, pose correctness, semantic completeness, physical connections or contact. `semantic_object_completeness` and `support_collision_truth` are null. The projected structure shows only 64 of 2,490 and 64 of 466 objects respectively; omitted dependencies cannot be certified and AABB relations are not contact truth (`r1/scene_rsi_pilot/C2/diagnostic_observations.json`, `initial_diagnoses[*].observation` and `probe_result` [0,12942)).

## Proposed mechanism versus execution and acceptance

The executed planning record says its plan was used before proposal, and calls for inventory/ownership, actual support surfaces, camera anchors, local material preservation and composite movement checks (`r1/scene_rsi_pilot/C2/meta_execution.json`, `plan`, `actually_used_for_proposal` [0,20132)). The accompanying `policy.py` describes a pre-execution review, render-dependent feedback and selection of successful steps; `r2/generic_rsi/C2/policy.py` additionally describes procedural texture, lighting, camera and primitive-budget heuristics. These are **program instructions**, not student execution traces proving those actions occurred. No supplied record establishes a pose-specific correction or lighting-quality result. [Members: `r1/scene_rsi_pilot/C2/policy.py` [0,6998); `r2/generic_rsi/C2/policy.py` [0,8703).]

The SceneEvolve teacher specifies visual score q from clipped SSIM, LPIPS and DINO; missing project or PNG scores zero, averaged over four development scenes. It requires retaining the best Selection-eligible H including H0, while prohibiting evaluator, split and budget changes (`r2/sceneevolve_cli/C2/meta_execution.json`, `plan.instruction` [0,4492)). The r1 functional record also states an expected absent composite fails rather than becoming N/A. These statements describe rules and selected test outcomes, **not a complete acceptance decision**: no acceptance thresholds, candidate comparison or acceptance result are supplied here.

## Audit findings

**Scope and citations.** All references are to archive `/srv/lab-disks/disk2/users/xymo/Documents/Codex/2026-11-02/scene-rsi-rsi-1-3d-blender/work/scene_archive/bundles/ablation_r1--generic_same_structured_evidence.tar.gz`. Member paths below are relative to it. The supplied records are selected records, not the complete archive; no images or student trajectories are provided for independent visual or execution verification.

**Detection versus hypothesis.** In `experiments/scene_rsi_pilot/candidates/ablation_r1/generic_same_structured_evidence/C1/diagnostic_observations.json` [0,4078), `initial_diagnoses[0]` records metric-measured reference/render disagreement for `commons_109923183`, with `code_location: unknown`; camera framing or object scale is explicitly a hypothesis. Its `probes` report completed single interventions: focal length 51 to 56.1 with geometry unchanged, and 1.1 scaling of the selected `arm gold-1` group around its geometry AABB center with camera fixed and non-targets preserved. Overall scores changed from 0.390749 to 0.387533 and 0.390962 respectively. These are scoped sensitivities, not proof of a camera cause, whole-object scale, or 3D accuracy. C2’s corresponding `diagnostic_observations.json` [0,4078) reports essentially the same results.

**Technical versus quality failure.** The proposal’s `diagnosis` claims both supplied trajectories first failed on excessive fillet radius, then missing Blender world after `realize`, and first rendered successfully on execution three; it also claims remaining framing, silhouette, occlusion and tonal discrepancies. Those are teacher claims, not independently checkable trajectory or image observations in this selection. The fixed visual scores do substantiate measured disagreement, not its visible character. The proposed `rationale` treats earlier successful builds and more visual revisions as a testable hypothesis, not an established improvement. [Candidate C1 `proposal_result.json` [0,23498), keys `diagnosis`, `rationale`, `scope`.]

**Proposed mechanism and its limits.** C1’s proposed H files instruct a complete first build with major objects, economical detail, reference-based revision, and safeguards for fillets, unique include keys, world creation, mesh naming and shared materials. Its `policy.py` [0,5449) is designed to execute at most three builds, distinguish an `ok` result with an image from failure, feed a render back, and permit selection only from successful rendered steps. The proposed `meta.py` [0,7545), keys `_diagnose` and `plan`, indexes execution failures and unknowns, prioritizes missing output, and interprets probes at their selected scope. The pre-proposal `meta_execution.json` [0,1788), `actually_used_for_proposal: true`, documents the earlier plan’s use; it does not show the new M or H executing on a student task. C2’s `meta.py` [0,4033), `plan`, combines generic instructions with structured diagnoses and priorities, while C2’s `policy.py` [0,1537) has a simpler execute-and-feedback loop. Code describes mechanisms; it is not evidence that a student followed them.

**Coverage, materials and relationships.** Multi-object coverage is an explicit construction goal, not a verified outcome. Procedural texture is allowed, and copying shared materials before local edits is prescribed; neither proves actual texture quality or non-target preservation. Grouping parts, unique keys, explicit pivots and snap/bind anchors address editability and relationships, while AABB anchors are expressly not collision/contact tests. Camera, light and render review are also instructions rather than verified visual corrections. [C1 `proposal_result.json`, `files[path=system.txt].content`; C1 `policy.py` [0,5449).] Pose or physical contact accuracy is not established by these records.

**Acceptance and substantiation.** The proposed M says this is an exploration branch, not acceptance of H; selection aggregates inform retention only, and missing output is failure. C1’s eight supplied result summaries—two development, two selection and four test—each report `structure_pass`, `material_pass`, `composite_pass` and `functional_pass` true, with `composite_required: true`. Their `interpretation` says an absent expected composite fails and passes mean structural/edit operability only; `semantic_object_completeness` and `support_collision_truth` are null. Thus the summaries substantiate reported functional passes, not semantic coverage, contact, visual quality or candidate acceptance. ## Audit conclusion

The selected records document diagnostic planning, some completed controlled probes and prescribed edit regressions, but **not an accepted improvement**. The reported candidate proposals failed technically; the supplied files do not establish that their student-side procedures ran or improved reconstruction quality.

**Citation convention:** Archive `/srv/lab-disks/disk2/users/xymo/Documents/Codex/2026-11-02/scene-rsi-rsi-1-3d-blender/work/scene_archive/bundles/history--service_incident_01.tar.gz`; all member paths below are relative to `experiments/scene_rsi_pilot/service_incident_01/proposal_context_before_retry/candidates/`. Character ranges are half-open.

### Detection and diagnosis

- In `ablation_r2/generic_same_structured_evidence/C1/diagnostic_observations.json` [0,1052), `initial_diagnoses` reports no valid output for `commons_109923183`, a technical/output failure, and metric-measured reference/render disagreement for `commons_118588033`/`bunks`, a quality problem with unresolved cause. Camera framing or object scale is explicitly a **hypothesis**, not attribution; code locations are unknown. Although the latter diagnosis says intervention is awaited, `probes` is empty. The generic planner selects probes only if the first scene is complete, while importing diagnoses from its structured planner; its executed plan confirms the empty list (`meta.py` [0,4033), `plan`; `meta_execution.json` [0,2013), `plan.probes`).
- For `bunks`, completed focal and scale interventions are recorded elsewhere: focal 27.5→30.25 with geometry unchanged; scale 1→1.1 around the target AABB center, with fixed camera and reported non-target preservation. Overall scores were respectively 0.39264 and 0.39504 versus 0.39655 baseline. These are scoped sensitivity observations, not proof of camera error, optimal pose or 3D accuracy (`r2/scene_rsi_pilot/C2/diagnostic_observations.json` [0,12287), `probes`, `result`, `visual_metrics`, `baseline_visual_metrics`). Lighting is not isolated by these probes.
- The richer teacher record reports failed execution events at steps 1 and 3 with empty stderr; API-error prevention is a hypothesis, not an established cause (`r1/scene_rsi_pilot/C2/diagnostic_observations.json` [0,12942), `initial_diagnoses[0]`). It also lists omission, overwrite, crop, occlusion and low contrast as alternative hypotheses; isolation was not run. No physical support or contact conclusion follows.

### Coverage, connections and appearance

The same record reports prescribed Blender material and movement tests passing for selected `apron_-1` and `architecture` groups. The material test copies target meshes/materials and replaces the surface with a blue shader; the move test reports preserved selected-part relative transforms and directly checked non-target state. These results support **those operations**, not original texture fidelity, semantic completeness, correct pose or contact. Each functional record explicitly leaves completeness and support/collision truth null. Projected structure shows only 64 of 2,490 and 64 of 466 object rows respectively; omitted rows cannot certify dependencies. AABB relations are not contact truth (`r1/scene_rsi_pilot/C2/diagnostic_observations.json` [0,12942), `initial_diagnoses`, `dependencies.coverage`, `probe_result.material`, `probe_result.move`).

### Proposed mechanism versus execution

The executed teacher plan prioritizes recovery before visual diagnosis and proposes checking ownership, local material preservation, relative transforms, camera anchors and construction versus occlusion (`r1/scene_rsi_pilot/C2/meta_execution.json` [0,20132), `plan.diagnoses`, `checks`, `instruction`, `actually_used_for_proposal`). Its corresponding policy contains a pre-execution review, error-specific feedback and selection among successful renders; this is **controller code**, not evidence that a student followed it or that a revision rendered (`r1/scene_rsi_pilot/C2/policy.py` [0,6998), `solve`). Other policy code asks for major-object coverage, procedural surface patterns and separate camera/material-light adjustments, but likewise does not establish the resulting scene (`r2/generic_rsi/C2/policy.py` [0,8703), `PROCEDURE`).

### Rules and outcome

The SceneEvolve teacher states a clipped SSIM/LPIPS/DINO score averaged over four development scenes, zero for a missing project or valid PNG, fixed backend and limits, and retention of the best Selection-eligible H including H0 (`r2/sceneevolve_cli/C2/meta_execution.json` [0,4492), `plan.instruction`). The structured guidance says expected composite absence is failure and fixed functional gates remain external; the selected records do not give a complete candidate-selection threshold or demonstrate its application (`r1/scene_rsi_pilot/C2/diagnostic_observations.json` [0,12942), `functional`; `ablation_r2/ours_without_regression_guidance/C1/meta_execution.json` [0,2509), `plan.instruction`).

All supplied pre-retry proposal results report `proposal_failed`, proposer exit 1 and `M_changed: false`, including `r1/scene_rsi_pilot/C2/proposal_result.json` [0,324) and `r2/sceneevolve_cli/C2/proposal_result.json` [0,320). The stderr contents and any candidate acceptance decision are not supplied. Thus neither acceptance nor an ablation benefit is substantiated; existing parent H/M files and completed probes should not be mistaken for a successful new proposal.

## Scope and evidence

This is an audit of selected records, not the whole archive. All citations refer to archive `/srv/lab-disks/disk2/users/xymo/Documents/Codex/2026-11-02/scene-rsi-rsi-1-3d-blender/work/scene_archive/bundles/replay_s1_r1_M_initial--frozen_meta.tar.gz`, under member prefix `experiments/scene_rsi_pilot/candidates/replay_s1_r1_M_initial/frozen_meta/C1/`. The supplied `evidence_projection.json` [0,201), keys `changes` and `selection_criterion`, reports no projection changes and selection by size and fixed source order, not score; it does not make the supplied records the entire archive.

## Detection, diagnosis and intervention

The teacher’s recorded detection was fixed-metric reference/render disagreement for `commons_2238909`, `bank_00`; the code location was initially unknown. Camera framing or object scale was explicitly a hypothesis, not an attribution (`diagnostic_observations.json` [0,5689), `initial_diagnoses`).

Two completed one-factor probes were recorded. Focal length 30→33, with geometry unchanged, reduced overall score from 0.36350 to 0.35505. Scaling the selected bank group by 1.1 about its geometry AABB center, with fixed camera and reported non-target preservation, reduced it to 0.35491; component metrics moved in mixed directions. Both records explicitly caution against causal or true-3D conclusions (`diagnostic_observations.json` [0,5689), `probes[*].result`, `visual_metrics`, `baseline_visual_metrics`). These are quality measurements, not execution failures or proof that an opposite change would improve quality.

The proposal additionally claims different exposure of a narrow book opening and hypothesizes occlusion or relative placement rather than omission. That is a teacher interpretation, not independently established image appearance here. Its claimed 1,189 approximate support and 432 overlap candidates have null semantic-support and true-collision labels; they do not establish bad contact or collisions (`proposal_result.json` [0,17710), `diagnosis`).

## Proposed mechanism versus observed execution

`meta_execution.json` [0,2036), keys `plan`, `actually_used_for_proposal` and `phase`, records that the initial M plan was used before proposal. The plan requested camera/scale probes and regression guidance while keeping objectives and acceptance fixed; `meta.py` [0,2694), `plan`, chooses the first complete development scene and orders functional failure categories. The proposal says all functional failure counts were zero, so the recorded priority order is not evidence of differential failure rates.

The proposed shared H is `policy.py`, not a scene-specific program. Its `GUIDANCE` calls for major-object coverage, stable groups, checking occlusion before adding geometry, explicit support anchors and composite descendants; it cautions that AABB overlap is not collision truth. For materials it recommends narrow subtree targeting and copying shared materials before local shader edits. Camera pose, focal length and local scale are separated as hypotheses; lighting/exposure is distinguished from albedo. The execution guidance addresses world initialization, unique keys and valid geometry, and treats missing output as failure (`policy.py` [0,13300), `GUIDANCE`). These are proposed instructions, not evidence that all objects, textures, connections or poses were correctly reconstructed.

`policy.py` [0,13300), `solve`, defines a student mechanism: execute exactly one returned Python block, require `ok`, an image and a step for a usable render, preserve successful steps after failure, and allow selection among successful renders. This is executable logic, but the supplied records contain no student execution trace demonstrating that it ran. The proposal reports only `policy.py` replacement, `M_changed: false`, `M_strategy_structure_changed: false`, `status: proposed`, and says no independent execution or evaluation was performed (`proposal_result.json` [0,17710), `files`, `rationale`, `scope`, `status`). Thus there is no evidenced M update or accepted candidate here.

## Acceptance and limitations

The policy’s usable-render rule is a technical validity gate, not a quality acceptance rule. Its `REVIEW` and `_selection_content` ask for comparison on framing, silhouettes, openings, occlusion, color regions and editable structure, without assuming the newest build is best (`policy.py` [0,13300)). The fixed evaluator’s actual thresholds and a candidate-acceptance decision are not supplied. The proposal reports passing structure/material/composite checks and non-target preservation, but these are proposal claims in this selection, not independently inspectable acceptance records. Neither those claims nor successful rendering would establish semantic completeness, physical contact, texture fidelity or metric 3D accuracy.

Its listed Box and Rod descendants passed prescribed operations: a copied-material blue shader edit preserved 1,263 checked non-target objects; a move preserved part relations within a reported maximum matrix delta of 2.384185791015625e-07 and preserved 1,217 checked non-target objects. These are reported operation results, not proof of texture fidelity, all-object coverage or physical contact (`diagnostic_observations.json` [0,6915), `initial_diagnoses[0].probe_result`).

The dependency projection shows 64 of 1,308 objects; its empty observed shared-material peer list cannot certify the 1,244 omitted rows. AABB relations likewise cannot establish contact or collision. The record explicitly leaves support and completeness unknown, so neither the move pass nor owner membership proves a correct pose or support surface (`diagnostic_observations.json` [0,6915), `initial_diagnoses[0].observation.dependencies`).

**Proposed update, not demonstrated student behavior.** The pre-proposal meta plan was actually used and requested no probes; it prioritizes composite, structure and material review. Its code allocates new probes only with explicit budget/count evidence, and warns against causal conclusions from a single controlled delta (`meta_execution.json` [0,11985), `plan.probes`, `plan.regression_priorities`, `actually_used_for_proposal`; `meta.py` [0,11051), `plan`). The proposed reusable policy recommends semantic-owner inventories, unique include keys, descendant-based shader targeting and local material copies; owner-relative transforms and actual support surfaces; multi-depth camera anchors; and separating lighting/exposure from surface color. It keeps renderer settings fixed and treats anchor estimates as hypotheses (`policy.py` [0,13668), `GUIDANCE`). These are instructions, not evidence that a student followed them or that appearance improved.

Successful steps may be retained with `DONE` or `SELECT`; later renders are not automatically judged better (`policy.py` [0,13668), `solve`, `_chosen`). The reported development executions succeeded, so the proposal does not establish a technical output failure there; the reported visual discrepancies are quality concerns, not measured causal diagnoses.

For functional acceptance, the supplied record says an expected but absent composite counts as failure, never N/A; the selected target’s three checks passed. That substantiates only the recorded structural/edit-operability results, not semantic completeness, support, pose or visual quality. No candidate acceptance decision or actual student trajectory executing the proposed policy is supplied. ## Scope and control

This audit uses only the supplied selected records, not the full archive or images. The fixed teacher schedule requested no probes; both diagnostic files contain empty diagnoses and probes. Both meta executions say the plan was actually used before proposal. The sequential adaptation calls for one shared candidate and retaining the best Selection-eligible harness including H0; the underlying template otherwise says two candidates. [Members `candidates/r1/sceneevolve_cli/C1/diagnostic_observations.json` and `C2/diagnostic_observations.json`, each [0,46); `C1/meta_execution.json` and `C2/meta_execution.json`, each [0,4492), keys `plan`, `actually_used_for_proposal`.]

## Detection, hypotheses and proposed changes

**C1 teacher report, not independent image observation:** two supplied development outputs scored 0.39075 and 0.34796, passed structural checks, but reportedly differed in silhouette, viewpoint, framing, foreground placement, lighting and color; both reportedly showed grain. The teacher reports two failed builds followed by one successful render per trajectory, and repeated include keys silently removing parts. Its hypothesis was that invalid fillet radii and missing worlds consumed the first two executions, preventing a visual correction cycle. The proposed harness adds source-hazard guidance and a conditional model-only pre-execution audit, then requests image comparison after a confirmed render. [Member `candidates/r1/sceneevolve_cli/C1/proposal_result.json` [0,12694), keys `diagnosis`, `rationale`; `C1/policy.py` [0,9704), `GUIDANCE`, `solve`.]

**C2 teacher report:** scores of 0.42826 and 0.44256 accompanied claimed weak textile appearance, an oversized rigid pillow, thin framing and a cushion/frame gap; the other scene reportedly had misplaced geometry, missing chair visibility and different illumination. A reported third execution timed out with 68.58 seconds available and no confirmed output; the selected chaise output reportedly had 2,068 meshes. C2 hypothesizes that expensive detail and the pre-render audit consumed useful time. It proposes economical primitives and procedural surface markings, source checks within responses, an optional post-render visual audit and a time reserve. These are diagnoses and proposals, not proof of visual causes or physical contact. [Member `candidates/r1/sceneevolve_cli/C2/proposal_result.json` [0,14472), keys `diagnosis`, `rationale`, `scope`; `C2/policy.py` [0,11643), `GUIDANCE`, `solve`.]

Both policies ask for all major visible objects, multipart groups and explicit camera/framing reasoning. C2 additionally calls for negative-space checks, editable assemblies, material targeting, separate camera versus local-geometry and lighting hypotheses, and avoiding simultaneous global material darkening and light reduction. Pivot guidance addresses assembly rotation, but neither record establishes a particular pose correction or connected physical support. Material and texture guidance does not establish successful texture reproduction. [Members `C1/policy.py` and `C2/policy.py`, ranges above.]

## Execution and acceptance limits

The policies describe mechanisms, not their observed execution in the supplied records: C1 conditionally audits before its first `runtime.execute`; C2 can audit after a successful render and preserve an incumbent using `BEST`. Both require `ok`, an image and a step for a successful render; `SELECT` must name a successful step, while `DONE` selects the latest success. C2’s final fallback is its incumbent, which is not necessarily visually reviewed if no valid `BEST` was supplied. No student trajectories or actual render feedback are included here to verify that these branches ran. [Members `C1/policy.py` [0,9704), `C2/policy.py` [0,11643), `solve`.]

Both proposal records report unchanged M and accepted **revalidation**, with `newly_eligible: false`; this is not evidence that either harness won Selection or improved over H0. C2 names C1 as parent. The template defines scene score as the mean of clipped SSIM, inverted clipped LPIPS and normalized DINOv2 cosine; missing project or valid PNG scores zero. The supplied records do not show the Selection comparison or final retention decision. [Members `C1/proposal_result.json` [0,12694) and `C2/proposal_result.json` [0,14472), keys `parent_source`, `revalidation`; `C1/meta_execution.json` [0,4492), `plan.instruction`.]

All supplied C1 and C2 development and Selection functional summaries, and C2 test summaries, report structural, material, composite and functional passes. Their own interpretation limits these to structural/edit operability; `semantic_object_completeness` and `support_collision_truth` are null. They therefore do not substantiate complete multi-object coverage, accurate appearance, or contact. ## Detection and diagnosis

- **Technical failure versus quality:** `r1/generic_rsi/C1/proposal_result.json`, `diagnosis`, reports two initial failures per development run: excessive fillet radius, then missing World, followed by a successful third render. It reports no remaining executable visual-correction opportunity. Repeated `include` keys and mesh-name material targeting are separate reported implementation hazards. Framing, coverage, silhouette and tone differences are its quality assessments, not consequences established by those failures.
- `r1/generic_rsi/C2/proposal_result.json`, `diagnosis`, reports five successful execution events and one timeout across two later cases. The timed-out correction produced no required output, so its intended changes were not validated. Its 1,864-versus-235 mesh comparison motivates a construction-cost hypothesis, but does not identify the timeout phase or prove causation.
- **Coverage and connections:** `r1/scene_rsi_pilot/C1/proposal_result.json`, `diagnosis`, reports compartment construction despite a hypothesis that foreground items were absent or cropped; omission, cropping and occlusion remain competing explanations. It also reports chaise upholstery outside its owner. `r2/scene_rsi_pilot/C2/proposal_result.json`, `diagnosis`, reports rack rails outside the tested rack despite passing edits. These are reported source findings, not proof of rendered visibility or physical disconnection.
- **Camera and pose:** The first pilot diagnosis reports a focal intervention from 51 to about 56.1 and overall 0.390748658 to 0.387274029; scaling only `arm gold-1` by 1.1 gave 0.390894127. The latter cannot establish whole-chaise scale. In `r2/scene_rsi_pilot/C2/proposal_result.json`, `diagnosis`, focal and selected-group scale interventions likewise have mixed metrics. Neither identifies a unique camera, object pose or true geometry.
- **Material, texture, light and render:** `r1/sceneevolve_cli/C2/proposal_result.json`, `diagnosis`, reports weak textile appearance and differing illumination; these are teacher visual assessments. Its proposed procedural surface patterns and scoped material edits are not evidence that texture improved. Missing renders and timeouts cannot support appearance claims.

## Proposed mechanisms versus execution

The proposed H policies call `runtime.ask`, execute complete programs, pass actual successful images back for review and select successful steps. For example, `r1/generic_rsi/C2/policy.py` [0,8390) adds a wall-time reserve, failure feedback and selection-only gate; this is **code for a mechanism**, not proof that a student followed it. Its `FIRST_BUILD` and `REVISION` prioritize multiple objects, solid surfaces, silhouette and camera/local corrections. `r1/scene_rsi_pilot/C2/policy.py` [0,12776), `PROCEDURE`, separates global lighting from local materials, preserves assemblies and treats AABB contact as unverified.

The proposed M in `r1/scene_rsi_pilot/C1/meta.py` [0,11051), `plan`, records observations, hypotheses, dependencies and unknowns separately and bounds probes. `r1/generic_rsi/C2/meta.py` [0,9423), `_execution_summary` and `plan`, separates regression renders from reconstruction executions and treats an omitted projected image key as unknown. These are proposed planning rules. The proposal records explicitly say no independent execution or rendering was performed in the proposer sessions; reported historical runs are not execution of the newly proposed candidates.

## Acceptance and limits

A selectable build under the cited policies requires a successful result and image, with a step identifier; this is distinct from candidate validation and visual quality. Prescribed structure/material/move passes test selected operations, not all objects, true contact or metric 3D accuracy. The fixed SceneEvolve meta-policy calls for retaining the best Selection-eligible H including H0, but does not specify a quality threshold in the supplied member (`r1/sceneevolve_cli/C1/meta.py` [0,596)).

Revalidation accepted `r1/scene_rsi_pilot/C1` after an earlier disallowed-import failure, with `newly_eligible: true` and no engineer source edit; `r1/generic_rsi/C1` was accepted but not newly eligible (their respective `proposal_result.json` keys `revalidation`). Conversely, `r2/generic_rsi/C2` reports `proposal_failed` for candidate introspection, and `r2/scene_rsi_pilot/C1` for a disallowed execution primitive. These statuses establish validation outcomes, not Selection retention or improved reconstruction quality. No supplied acceptance result establishes that the proposed updates improved images or generalized beyond the reported cases.

## Proposed changes and observed mechanism

C1 proposed reusable H/policy and M changes: a same-role pre-execution review, build-failure handling, successful-render selection, and dependency-aware probe/regression planning. Its `policy.py` implements those branches; C1 `meta_execution.json` says the **initial** meta plan was actually used before proposal. C2 proposed inline rather than separate review, execution-time accounting and fallback selection; its proposal says M was unchanged (C1 `policy.py` [0,6998); C1 `meta_execution.json` `source`, `actually_used_for_proposal`; C2 `meta_execution.json` `source`, `actually_used_for_proposal`; C2 `proposal_result.json` `rationale`, `M_changed`).

The shared instructions address multiple-object inventory, semantic owners and unique slots; copying shared materials for local edits; keeping attached parts and relative transforms together; and separating object pose/scale from camera and appearance. C2 adds light/world versus local-material distinctions and leaves runner render settings fixed. These are safeguards and hypotheses, not demonstrated texture fidelity, correct pose, illumination, or physical contact (C1 `proposal_result.json` [0,32641), `files` entries `system.txt` and `meta.py`; C2 `policy.py` [0,12776), `PROCEDURE`).

## Tests and acceptance limits

C2’s diagnostics report prescribed Blender material replacement and group movement for C1’s `apron_-1` and `architecture`: target success, non-target preservation and relative-transform checks passed, with three renders completed each. These are actual recorded test operations, not student text edits. The selected groups are only a subset: projected structure shows 64 of 2,490 and 64 of 466 rows, respectively. No observed shared-material peers in those projections cannot certify omitted dependencies; AABBs cannot establish contact. Completeness and support/collision truth remain null (C2 `diagnostic_observations.json` [0,12942), `initial_diagnoses` for both targets, especially `dependencies.coverage`, `probe_result.material`, `probe_result.move`).

The functional rule treats an expected absent composite as failure, not N/A. C1 selection `commons_133800864` has `structure_pass: false` and `functional_pass: false`; C2’s two selection summaries pass, as do its four supplied test summaries. These results substantiate selected-target operability, not whole-scene quality or coverage (results `C1/selection/commons_133800864/functional/summary.json`; `C2/selection/{commons_133800864,commons_138420790}/functional/summary.json`; `C2/test/{commons_155212263,commons_16007442,commons_163096530,commons_199974832}/functional/summary.json`, exact `*_pass`, `interpretation` and null truth keys).

Both proposal revalidations record `status: accepted`, but also `newly_eligible: false`; acceptance of the candidate record must not be conflated with demonstrated visual improvement. The supplied records do not specify a complete candidate-selection threshold or establish one from scores. Neither proposal claims independent validation of improvement (C1 and C2 `proposal_result.json`, `status`, `scope`, `revalidation`).

## Historical audit: B26, chunk 1 of 2

**Scope and citations.** This assessment uses only the supplied selected records, not the whole archive or the images themselves. All member paths below are inside `/srv/lab-disks/disk2/users/xymo/Documents/Codex/2026-11-02/scene-rsi-rsi-1-3d-blender/work/scene_archive/bundles/shared-evidence.tar.gz`, under `experiments/scene_rsi_pilot/`. JSON citations use exact keys and identifying values; character ranges are those supplied with each complete member.

### Detection, hypothesis and proposed changes

The initial teacher M distinguishes incomplete outputs from valid but visually discrepant reconstructions. It labels camera framing or object scale as a **hypothesis**, requests one-factor focal and scale probes, and leaves code location unknown. Its planned H change is to use probe sensitivity and dependency regressions, not attribute a cause from one score delta. The no-probe and no-regression-guidance controls explicitly suppress those respective inputs, while retaining fixed final objectives. [Members `initial_meta/scene_rsi_pilot/meta.py` [0,2694), `ablation_initial/ours_without_probes/meta.py` [0,2819), and `ablation_initial/ours_without_regression_guidance/meta.py` [0,2797), `plan`].

A generic teacher’s proposal **reports**, rather than independently establishes here, two failed builds per supplied trajectory: excessive fillet radius, then missing World, followed by a successful third render. It also reports duplicate include keys and incorrect material targeting. Its focal perturbation changed one score from 0.390749 to 0.387274; scaling a small trim group yielded 0.390894. Those target-limited results do not establish whole-object scale or optimal camera. The proposal replaces H and M with API checks, bounded selection and evidence-led probe scheduling; its own `scope` says the replacements were not independently executed by the proposer. [Member `validation_correction_v2/candidates/r1/generic_rsi/C1/proposal_result.json` [0,20854), `diagnosis`, `rationale`, `scope`, `files`].

The SceneEvolve C2 teacher **claims** silhouette, cushion/frame gap, weak textile appearance, misplaced geometry and illumination discrepancies; these are teacher observations, not image findings independently verified in this audit. It reports a third execution timeout, not an improved render, and proposes economical geometry, procedural surface patterns and a reviewed fallback. [Member `validation_correction_v2/candidates/r1/sceneevolve_cli/C2/proposal_result.json` [0,13981), `diagnosis`, `rationale`, `files`].

### Mechanisms and their limits

The later shared H code asks for all major semantic objects and attached parts, unique slots, support and occlusion review, and dependent-part movement after pose changes. It separates local material changes from world/light changes and requests multiple camera anchors. These are **instructions**, not proof that a student followed them or that objects touched. Its executable loop calls `runtime.execute`, accepts a fallback only with `ok`, an image and an integer step, and selects successful steps; a timeout alone does not identify the slow phase. [Member `external_followup_inputs/bw_0011/scene_rsi_pilot/policy.py` [0,12776), `PROCEDURE`, `solve`].

Actual recorded prescribed operations provide narrower evidence: for `apron_-1` and `architecture`, material replacement and composite movement passed with non-target preservation and relative-transform checks. These are selected groups, not verification of every object, texture or connection. The summaries show only 64 of 2,490 and 64 of 466 objects respectively; semantic completeness and support/collision truth remain null. AABBs cannot establish physical contact. [Member `reports/summary.json` [0,252451), `paired_analysis.M_descendants_actually_used`, candidate `candidates/r1/scene_rsi_pilot/C2`, `plan.diagnoses[].probe_result` and `dependencies.coverage`].

For actual student execution, the follow-up record reports two executions and a complete pilot reconstruction, despite a subsequent student-call timeout; its judge failed. H0 had three executions but missing required artifacts and no valid quality score. Thus the timeout and missing output are technical failures, distinct from measured visual quality. [Same summary member, `additional_stages.external_followup.records`, task `bw_0011`, `reconstruction.usage`, `runtime_status`, `metrics`, `adapted_evaluation`].

### Acceptance and strength of evidence

The stated selection policy retains the best Selection-eligible H, including H0; the fixed teacher specifies missing Blender project or PNG as zero and a visual score combining clipped SSIM, LPIPS and DINOv2. Functional passes test prescribed edit operability, not scene truth. The supplied records do not fully specify every eligibility threshold, so rejection reasons should not be reconstructed solely from correlations. [Same summary member, `paired_analysis.M_descendants_actually_used`, candidate `candidates/r1/sceneevolve_cli/C2`, `plan.instruction`].

Pilot r1 C1 was rejected with one of two Selection functional passes; its descendant M nevertheless guided C2, which was accepted with two passes and Selection quality 0.43955. Pilot r2 C1 failed proposal validation and C2 was rejected; H0 was selected. Exploration-branch M use is therefore not itself H acceptance. No method achieved complete valid improvement across either scheduled repeat, and ablation conditions had no common valid scene set. These records do not substantiate general meta-capability improvement. [Same summary member, `paired_analysis.candidates`, `M_descendants_actually_used`, `complete_valid_improvement`, `additional_stage_analysis.ablation_coverage`, and `selections`].

## Visual audit — VX02

**Sources:** Image 1, reference: `/srv/lab-disks/disk2/users/xymo/Documents/Codex/2026-11-02/scene-rsi-rsi-1-3d-blender/outputs/scene_rsi_v2_1/results/r1/fixed_h0/H0/development/commons_187101683/reference.png`; Image 2, first available rendered step: `/srv/lab-disks/disk2/users/xymo/Documents/Codex/2026-11-02/scene-rsi-rsi-1-3d-blender/outputs/scene_rsi_v2_1/results/r1/fixed_h0/H0/development/commons_187101683/steps/002/final.png`; Image 3, final selected render: `/srv/lab-disks/disk2/users/xymo/Documents/Codex/2026-11-02/scene-rsi-rsi-1-3d-blender/outputs/scene_rsi_v2_1/results/r1/fixed_h0/H0/development/commons_187101683/final.png`.

### Directly visible observations

- **Coverage and arrangement:** Images 2 and 3 retain the reference’s main composition: a warm-colored sofa behind a dark coffee table, two white cups with saucers and flat rectangular items, a central potted flowering plant, and a curtain at right. They also show two sofa pillows. The plant, cups, and table overlap in a broadly similar front-to-back arrangement to Image 1, although the rendered sofa fills more of the frame and the table is more heavily cropped.
- **Visible parts and pose:** In Image 1 the sofa has rounded, irregular upholstery, visible cushion separations, broad arms, and a textured lower front. In Images 2 and 3 its cushions and arms are smoother, squarer and more uniformly defined. The left pillow is more upright and rectangular in the renders; the right pillow is also more distinctly outlined. The two cups flank the plant in all three images, but their apparent positions and handle orientations differ. Overlap and occlusion prevent confirmation of physical contact or hidden parts.
- **Materials and detail:** The reference sofa has mottled, velvety-looking shading and creases; the rendered sofa has a more even, peach-colored surface. The reference table shows subtle surface variation, while the rendered table is flatter and uniformly dark. The plant in the renders reproduces green foliage and pale blue flower clusters, though the flowers and leaves appear simplified and less naturalistic. The reference curtain has broad, soft folds and reflective highlights; the rendered curtain has narrower, more regular vertical folds.
- **Camera and lighting:** Image 1 shows more of the sofa’s front and table legs, with stronger depth perspective and a darker foreground. Images 2 and 3 have a more frontal, elevated-looking view of the sofa and tabletop; the table extends beyond the right and lower image boundaries. The reference has directional light, pronounced shadow and shallow-looking background softness. The renders are brighter and more evenly lit, with conspicuous grain and less nuanced shading.
- **First and final:** Images 2 and 3 appear identical at the supplied resolution. The visible evidence therefore does not establish a change between these two supplied renders; it does not rule out unshown attempts or changes.

### Geometric hypotheses and limits

The differing framing could reflect camera placement, focal length, object proportions, or a combination; the images alone do not identify which. Apparent gaps or overlaps around cushions, cups, saucers and the pot do not prove whether surfaces touch. Occlusion and render noise also limit assessment of small details and hidden object count. This deterministic small sample is not a conclusion about all Scene RSI experiments, nor does it establish diagnosis, adoption, or causal improvement.

## Limited visual audit — VX05, scene_rsi_v3, bw_0017

### Directly visible observations

**Coverage and detail.** The reference (image 1) shows an industrial hall with several large silver processing units, raised platforms, yellow guardrails and staircases, a green vertical tank, and extensive foreground conveyor equipment. The first render (image 2) includes the hall structure, tank, yellow stairs and rails, and blue conveyor-like foreground surfaces, but its roof and elevated viewpoint obscure much of the machinery area. The final render (image 3) makes the raised machinery and walkway more visible: two light-colored machine masses and duct-like arches are discernible. It still represents fewer small components and less of the dense foreground equipment visible in the reference. Occlusion and image resolution limit any count of parts.

The reference has varied metallic reflections, detailed rails, rollers, pipes and machine faces. Both renders use simpler surfaces and repeated linear elements. In image 3, the dark slatted machine face and blue foreground strips provide recognizable detail, but fine components and material variation remain less distinct. Visible grain in both renders further limits assessment.

**Adjacency and pose.** In image 1, stairs rise toward the guarded elevated machinery area; the green tank appears in front of it, with conveyors occupying the lower foreground. Images 2 and 3 retain a similar broad arrangement of tank, elevated yellow-railed area, stairs and foreground conveyors. In image 3, a staircase crosses visually in front of the tank area, and a tall stairway stands at the right end of the platform. These are screen-space relationships: the images alone do not establish physical contact or exact depth alignment.

**Camera and lighting.** Image 1 uses an interior, roughly eye-level oblique view, with the machinery prominent across the middle and bright directional highlights. Image 2 looks down from outside or above the hall boundary, so the broad roof dominates the upper image and hides the principal machinery. Image 3 moves to an interior, more frontal view, closer to the reference’s presentation, although its foreground conveyor is cropped and the composition and perspective remain different. Both renders are visibly noisy; image 2 also has a conspicuously bright roof patch, while image 3 has dark overhead areas and unevenly bright machinery. These rendering effects obscure some detail.

### Geometric hypotheses and limits

The railings, stairs and supports in the renders suggest an elevated platform, and the curved forms above the machines suggest ducts. Their precise geometry, connections and hidden components cannot be verified from these views. The final view reveals more machinery than the first available view, but the images do not establish why the views differ or demonstrate a causal improvement, teacher diagnosis or student adoption.

# S01 audit: D01 stagewise reconstruction

## Scope and conclusion

This audit uses only the supplied text records for one development trajectory, D01. No reference image, render, Blender checkpoint, or other experiment was independently inspected. Visual findings below are **teacher claims**, not direct observations by this auditor. The records support targeted, executed incremental edits and several teacher-reported partial improvements; they do **not** establish reference-level reconstruction quality or a learned capability gain.

For compact citations, **P** denotes the exact directory `/srv/lab-disks/disk2/users/xymo/Documents/Codex/2026-11-02/scene-rsi-rsi-1-3d-blender/outputs/stagewise_teaching_student_selfcheck_pilot`. Paths written as `P/...` identify files under that absolute directory; JSON citations name actual keys. Stage checkpoints are successive versions of the same scene, not independent experiments. `P/tasks/D01/state.json` keys `split`, `status`, `stage_index`, and `last_checkpoint` record development completion at stage 5, not quality acceptance. `P/protocol_freeze.json` records `automatic_quality_evaluation: false`.

The plan divides work into shell/viewpoint, furniture, plants/fixtures, desktop details, and appearance/global check (`P/tasks/D01/plan.json`, `stages`). The reported completion scopes are assertions, not independent proof that every reference object or relationship was correctly reconstructed.

## Findings by concern

### Multi-object coverage and plants

- **Furniture:** The teacher said all four stage-2 targets were present, while identifying a chair-proportion issue. Executed construction code defines a desk, two visitor chairs, rear chair, and cabinetry with explicit positions (`P/tasks/D01/stages/02/initial_build/patch.py`, lines 14–55; `P/tasks/D01/stages/02/stage.json`, `review`). The build event records 458 created scene objects, which are not 458 semantic objects (`P/tasks/D01/stages/02/initial_build/stage_events.jsonl`, event 5). Presence of modeled categories supports coverage, not accurate reference placement or completeness.
- **Plants:** The teacher reported no visible foreground plant and sparse rear foliage, but explicitly could not distinguish off-frame from occlusion. Thus missing geometry was **not** a supported diagnosis. The targeted revision translated the foreground parent and generated 260 rear and 300 foreground leaves; execution records two creations and 33 modifications (`P/tasks/D01/stages/03/visual_revision_build/patch.py`, lines 6–10, 14–24, 36–45, 63–65; `P/tasks/D01/stages/03/visual_revision_build/stage_events.jsonl`, event 5). The teacher then reported foreground visibility and a fuller but still narrow, transparent rear canopy; the radiator remained identifiable at that stage (`P/tasks/D01/stages/03/stage.json`, `review`, `confirmation`). This is partial teacher verification, not proof of botanical fidelity. Later framing reportedly made the foreground plant obscure the radiator, a regression rather than stable multi-object visibility (`P/tasks/D01/stages/05/stage.json`, `confirmation.findings`, F1).

### Part proportions, disconnected parts, and support

- **Visitor chairs:** The teacher described upholstery too close to the floor and short visible stems, not a proven physical disconnection. The targeted, executed patch scales both backs around their top anchor, raises seats and arms by 0.20, and extends stems about their lower anchor (`P/tasks/D01/stages/02/visual_revision_build/patch.py`, lines 4–22). Scene-change records show corresponding chair components modified, without creations or deletions (`P/tasks/D01/stages/02/visual_revision_build/stage_events.jsonl`, event 5; `P/tasks/D01/stages/02/stage.json`, `student_revision.measured_scene_changes`). The teacher marked the visible proportion issue resolved but noted noise prevented precise floor-contact assessment (`stage.json`, `confirmation`). Resolution of appearance is not verification of mechanical attachment.
- **Monitor support:** The teacher reported a poorly separated monitor silhouette and requested contrast, orientation, and a stand seated on the desk; washout versus occlusion was explicitly uncertain. The adopted patch changes housing material and rotates the panel and bezel, **not the stand** (`P/tasks/D01/stages/04/visual_revision_build/patch.py`, lines 5–16, 23–34; `P/tasks/D01/stages/04/stage.json`, `student_revision.measured_scene_changes`). The teacher reported improved panel visibility but could not verify support contact (`stage.json`, `confirmation`). Neither a disconnected stand nor its correction is established. Material and orientation changed together, so their individual causal effects cannot be isolated.

### Position, orientation, camera, and composition

- **Stage 1:** The teacher identified an upper black strip, a distant-looking back wall, and excessive foreground floor, while acknowledging camera and room dimensions are not uniquely determined. The targeted revision actually changed the existing camera location, aim, and lens (`P/tasks/D01/stages/01/visual_revision_build/patch.py`, lines 5–12; `P/tasks/D01/stages/01/visual_revision_build/stage_events.jsonl`, event 5). The teacher reported the strip gone and the back-floor junction moving from roughly 63% to 75% image height, but the opening reached the left edge and the foreground wall was largely lost; ceiling-panel visibility remained uncertain (`P/tasks/D01/stages/01/stage.json`, `review`, `confirmation`). These percentages are teacher estimates, not independent measurements.
- **Stage 5:** The teacher again reported inadequate left-wall coverage, small furniture, misplaced ceiling-light framing, and a dark top strip; the strip’s cause was uncertain. The update was targeted: camera position/lens and ceiling scale changed, with measured scene changes supporting adoption (`P/tasks/D01/stages/05/visual_revision_build/patch.py`, lines 3–14; `P/tasks/D01/stages/05/stage.json`, `student_revision.measured_scene_changes`). The teacher reported larger furniture farther right, but the left wall and dark strip remained problematic, the right chair was clipped, and foliage obscured the radiator (`stage.json`, `confirmation.findings`, F1). This is mixed and partly regressive, not a validated general camera fix.

### Materials, texture, lighting, and rendering

Stage 5’s teacher reported pale floor and walls, insufficient chair contrast, and heavy noise; the teacher could not reliably assess oak grain, fabric texture, or contact shadows. The initial appearance patch actually created procedural oak grain, assigned it to named cabinet meshes, changed the floor material and reduced three lights (`P/tasks/D01/stages/05/initial_build/patch.py`, lines 9–19, 21–53; `P/tasks/D01/stages/05/stage.json`, `initial_response.measured_scene_changes`). Those changes are code/tool evidence of implementation, not visible texture fidelity. The teacher said the initial and prior attachments appeared unchanged, so their visual effect was not confirmed.

The subsequent feedback-targeted patch changed floor and wall materials, assigned darker material to the right chair, and reduced two lights; measured changes support adoption (`P/tasks/D01/stages/05/visual_revision_build/patch.py`, lines 16–57; `P/tasks/D01/stages/05/stage.json`, `student_revision.measured_scene_changes`). The teacher reported a darker olive chair and warmer wall, but a pale floor and persistent noise; texture and subtle shadows remained unverified (`stage.json`, `confirmation.findings`, F2). A cleaner render was requested, but the student stated the fixed runner controlled render cleanliness; no cleaner-render adoption is evidenced. The final event log records Cycles CPU, 768 × 513, 16 samples, and render completion—not a quality score (`P/tasks/D01/stages/05/visual_revision_build/stage_events.jsonl`, events 11–13).

### Technical failure

Stage 4 initially failed before producing a checkpoint or render: the monitor-foot fillet radius 0.015 exceeded half its 0.025 minimum dimension. The initial event log stops at build start (`P/tasks/D01/stages/04/initial_build/patch.py`, line 8; `P/tasks/D01/stages/04/initial_build/stage_events.jsonl`, events 1–4; `P/tasks/D01/stages/04/stage.json`, `attempts`). The repair reduced that radius to 0.012 and the mouse radius to 0.017 (`P/tasks/D01/stages/04/technical_repair_build/patch.py`, lines 8–10). Its events report build and render completion (`P/tasks/D01/stages/04/technical_repair_build/stage_events.jsonl`, events 5 and 13). This was technical recovery, not evidence of a teacher-diagnosed visual improvement.

## Acceptance and transfer limits

The supplied records do not define or report an H/M acceptance rule or an H/M result; none can be inferred. Here, `advance`, `advance_with_issue`, `resolved`, and task `completed` are recorded workflow or teacher-verification statuses, not proof of true learned capability. Four of five stage statuses are `advance_with_issue` (`P/tasks/D01/stages/01/stage.json` through `P/tasks/D01/stages/05/stage.json`, `status`).

The frozen self-check policy asks about framing, visibility, parts, tonal readability, and carryover (`P/transfer/self_check_policy.md`, lines 1–8). `P/transfer/lesson_provenance.json`, `rules` and `limitations`, attributes these lessons to this single trajectory and explicitly says the Python entry constructs a prompt rather than measuring geometry or verifying execution. `P/transfer/freeze.json` records `test_access: false`, `test_started: false`, and `is_weight_training: false`. Consequently, the evidence supports a frozen language-review strategy extracted from D01, not demonstrated transfer, weight learning, teacher self-evolution, or improved reconstruction capability on unseen cases.

# S02 audit: stagewise T01

## Scope and conclusion

The supplied records show one incremental test trajectory, not ten independent reconstructions: five semantic stages, each with an initial build and one visual revision, linked through parent checkpoints. The task state says `completed`, but every stage says `advance_with_issue`. The final record does **not** establish a high-quality reconstruction: the student’s final self-verification reports remaining backdrop, framing, and appearance problems. No images were supplied for this audit, so those visual findings are the student’s recorded claims, not independent observations.

For compact citations, **P** denotes the exact absolute directory `/srv/lab-disks/disk2/users/xymo/Documents/Codex/2026-11-02/scene-rsi-rsi-1-3d-blender/outputs/stagewise_teaching_student_selfcheck_pilot`. Paths below append to P; JSON citations identify actual keys. The supplied patch files and event logs were reviewed as text, not the referenced `.blend` or image files.

The supplied T01 reviews are explicitly `student_self_review` and `student_self_verification`, **not teacher reviews**. Teacher/development feedback appears only through the summarized provenance in `P/transfer/lesson_provenance.json`, `rules`; its visual outcomes are recorded claims. A frozen language policy was produced before test access, not a weight update: `P/transfer/freeze.json`, keys `source_task`, `test_access`, `is_weight_training`, and `is_teacher_self_evolution`. No H/M acceptance rule or H/M outcome is supplied here; neither acceptance under such a rule nor learned capability can be inferred. The provenance itself disclaims demonstrated capability improvement and automatic quality measurement (`P/transfer/lesson_provenance.json`, `limitations` and `not_transferred`).

## Concern-by-concern findings

### Multi-object coverage and position

- **Development feedback:** The teacher-associated provenance reports a foreground plant present in inventory but invisible and sparse rear foliage. A targeted development patch moved one plant and added foliage; recorded verification claimed improved foreground visibility but a still-narrow rear canopy. This supports checking inventory separately from recognition, not a universal foliage fix or a uniquely established cause (`P/transfer/lesson_provenance.json`, rules R3 and R6).
- **T01 diagnosis and adoption:** The stage-2 initial executed code creates an island top, carcass, four posts, panels and hardware (`P/tasks/T01/stages/02/initial_build/patch.py`, lines 66–94); stage 3 creates left and right cabinet groups (`P/tasks/T01/stages/03/initial_build/patch.py`, lines 53–98). The stage-2 build log records 39 creations, and stage 3 records 142 creations and one modification (`P/tasks/T01/stages/02/initial_build/stage_events.jsonl`, event 5; `P/tasks/T01/stages/03/initial_build/stage_events.jsonl`, event 5). These support actual scene coverage, not reference completeness or visibility.
- **Verification:** Stage-3 self-review says the left cabinet was compressed and the right heavily cropped; after a targeted camera-only revision, self-verification says coverage improved but a black margin and lower-cabinet occlusion remained (`P/tasks/T01/stages/03/stage.json`, `review`, `student_revision.measured_scene_changes`, `confirmation`). Stage 4’s camera revision enlarged the island from a claimed 49% to 59% of image width but reportedly still hid the left lower doors and newly cropped the right cabinetry (`P/tasks/T01/stages/04/stage.json`, `review`, `confirmation`). The changes targeted visibility, not missing geometry. Final self-verification still reports a narrow island end and cropped right cabinetry (`P/tasks/T01/stages/05/stage.json`, `confirmation.findings` F2). It cannot distinguish camera from geometry causes.

### Materials, texture and lighting

- **Development feedback:** Provenance describes pale/noisy surfaces and a combined material and light-energy update. Recorded verification reported a darker chair and warmer wall but a pale floor and persistent noise. Because factors changed together, it does not establish a general darkening or lighting remedy (`P/transfer/lesson_provenance.json`, rule R5).
- **T01 floor:** Stage-1 self-review claimed a pale, uniform floor and explicitly could not isolate material from lighting. The revision targeted both wall coverage and floor character; executed code adds position-driven noise and staggered seams (`P/tasks/T01/stages/01/visual_revision_build/patch.py`, lines 11–42 and 44–68), and the event log records one creation and 43 modifications (`P/tasks/T01/stages/01/visual_revision_build/stage_events.jsonl`, event 5). Student verification claims warmer texture and visible joints, still lighter and more uniform than the reference (`P/tasks/T01/stages/01/stage.json`, `review`, `confirmation`). That is partial self-reported improvement, not independently verified texture fidelity.
- **Cabinet wood and glass:** Stage-3 self-review reported pale wood and uniform glazing, but acknowledged lighting and exposure as alternatives to a material cause. Its visual revision was camera-only and deliberately deferred tone; measured changes support that distinction (`P/tasks/T01/stages/03/stage.json`, `review.findings` F2, `student_revision.measured_scene_changes`). Later stage-4 initial code changed glazing transmission and added shelves; stage-5 initial code reassigned procedural wood and a new reflective glass material (`P/tasks/T01/stages/04/initial_build/patch.py`, lines 22–33 and 52–64; `P/tasks/T01/stages/05/initial_build/patch.py`, lines 4–36 and 38–81). The measured stage-5 changes include material slots, so these were adopted edits, not merely claims. Nevertheless, final self-verification says the top remained pale with faint grain and glass mostly uniform cyan; shelf visibility and subtle reflections remained uncertain (`P/tasks/T01/stages/05/stage.json`, `initial_response.measured_scene_changes`, `confirmation.findings` F3). The final wall-only revision did not target those residual appearance issues.
- **Lighting/render settings:** Initial code sets two area emitters (`P/tasks/T01/stages/01/initial_build/patch.py`, lines 22–24); later cited revisions did not establish an independently tested lighting correction. The runner used Cycles, CPU, 768×576 and 16 samples, and completed the final render (`P/tasks/T01/stages/05/visual_revision_build/stage_events.jsonl`, events 6 and 11–13). Low-sample noise limits fine-detail assessment; render completion is technical success, not quality validation.

### Disconnected parts and contacts

Development feedback identified a hard-to-distinguish monitor panel and support. Its combined material/orientation update reportedly made the panel identifiable, but support contact remained unverified; recognition is not proof of connection (`P/transfer/lesson_provenance.json`, rules R4–R5).

In T01, no supplied teacher finding establishes a disconnected component. Stage-2 code places each 1.12-high post at z=0.56, so its nominal bottom is z=0; the stage-1 floor top is also nominally z=0. This supports intended floor contact in the initial program, not a full evaluated-geometry or reference-contact verification (`P/tasks/T01/stages/02/initial_build/patch.py`, lines 72–75; `P/tasks/T01/stages/01/initial_build/patch.py`, lines 9–11). Stage-4 initial code shortened rails and added shelves and recess uprights; execution records five creations and 50 modifications (`P/tasks/T01/stages/04/initial_build/patch.py`, lines 3–20 and 52–64; `P/tasks/T01/stages/04/initial_build/stage_events.jsonl`, event 5). The code comment’s diagnosis of dark artifacts from overlapping faces is a student hypothesis, not independently established visual causation. Final self-verification explicitly leaves obscured support contacts unverified (`P/tasks/T01/stages/05/stage.json`, `confirmation.findings` F2). Separate meshes or parenting alone do not prove a defect or a sound physical joint.

### Camera, backdrop and technical regression

Development feedback identified black strips and framing mismatches; recorded camera/ceiling changes had mixed outcomes, including new cropping. This supports regression checks, not a fixed camera recipe (`P/transfer/lesson_provenance.json`, rules R2 and R6).

In T01, the stage-2 self-review proposed a closer island view. The actual revision moved the camera to `(7, -6, 4.3)` and set a 52 mm lens; measured changes identify only the camera (`P/tasks/T01/stages/02/visual_revision_build/patch.py`, lines 4–11; `P/tasks/T01/stages/02/stage.json`, `student_revision.measured_scene_changes`). Although the render completed, the student’s confirmation describes it as almost entirely black with no recognizable island, while the inventory still listed its objects. The cause was not established in that confirmation (`P/tasks/T01/stages/02/stage.json`, `confirmation`). The next initial patch hid the right wall, stating that the camera was outside it; the original wall is centered at x=5.05 and the revised camera is at x=7, supporting that spatial premise, but not independently proving it caused the black image (`P/tasks/T01/stages/01/initial_build/patch.py`, line 16; `P/tasks/T01/stages/03/initial_build/patch.py`, lines 4–7). The hide-render change is measured in stage 3.

Backdrop correction remained incomplete. Stage 1 actually enlarged the back wall; stage 5 enlarged it again and its final targeted revision doubled horizontal scale (`P/tasks/T01/stages/01/visual_revision_build/patch.py`, lines 4–9; `P/tasks/T01/stages/05/visual_revision_build/patch.py`, lines 3–7). The final event log records one modification and render completion (`P/tasks/T01/stages/05/visual_revision_build/stage_events.jsonl`, events 5 and 13). Student verification claims the upper wedge was filled but another black triangular region remained at the left edge; its cause and world-space extent were uncertain. The same confirmation retains framing and appearance findings (`P/tasks/T01/stages/05/stage.json`, `confirmation`).

## Interpretation and limits

The record supports actual incremental edits, checkpoint continuation and technically completed renders, together with disciplined recording of several unresolved findings. It does not independently establish visual similarity, hidden contacts, or generalization from the development trajectory. The frozen protocol says automatic quality evaluation was off (`P/protocol_freeze.json`, `automatic_quality_evaluation`), and the transferred Python is described as prompt construction rather than geometry inspection or execution verification (`P/transfer/lesson_provenance.json`, `limitations`). Development provenance also notes an initial technical error and repair whose feedback identifier was unsupported; that rule was excluded rather than invented (`P/transfer/lesson_provenance.json`, `not_transferred`). Workflow completion, any hypothetical H/M threshold, and genuine learned reconstruction capability are separate claims; the supplied evidence establishes only the first for this task.
